A universal W-algebra for 𝓝 = 𝟒 \mathcal{N}=4 super Yang-Mills
Federico Bonetti, Carlo Meneghelli

Abstract
Using bootstrap methods, we provide evidence for the existence of a non-linear W-algebra, denoted 𝒲 ∞ s,s \mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}} , which contains the small 𝒩 = 4 \mathcal{N}=4 super Virasoro algebra and features an infinite tower of additional generators, organized in short supersymmetry multiplets. The algebra 𝒲 ∞ s,s \mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}} has one free parameter, its central charge c c . We claim that the simple quotient of 𝒲 ∞ s,s \mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}} for c = − 3 ​ ( N 2 − 1 ) c=-3(N^{2}-1) is isomorphic to the vertex operator algebra associated to 4d 𝒩 = 4 \mathcal{N}=4 𝔰 ​ 𝔲 ​ ( N ) \mathfrak{su}(N) super Yang-Mills theory. We define a filtration in 𝒲 ∞ s,s \mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}} and provide evidence that it reproduces the R R -filtration, which is crucial to extract 4d SCFT data from the vertex operator algebra. Finally, we present explicit formulae for all the (anti)commutators of the wedge algebra of 𝒲 ∞ s,s \mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}} .



1
Introduction and summary

To any 4d

\mathcal{N}=2

superconformal field theory
(SCFT) one can associate a vertex
operator algebra (VOA)
Beem:2013sza
,
which captures
a protected subsector of the 4d
SCFT.
This applies in particular to
4d
\mathcal{N}=4

\mathfrak{su}(N)
super Yang-Mills:
we denote its
associated VOA as
\mathcal{V}(A_{N-1})
.
Some properties of
\mathcal{V}(A_{N-1})
have been discussed in

Bonetti:2018fqz
, which proposes a
definition of this VOA in terms of a free field realization
based on the Weyl group of
\mathfrak{su}(N)
.
This viewpoint allows to formulate precise
conjectures on
\mathcal{V}(A_{N-1})
, some of which
have been proven in
Arakawa:2023cki
.

Motivated by the existence of the 1-parameter family of VOAs

\mathcal{V}(A_{N-1})
,
in this work we address the following question:
is there a universal W-algebra
for the sequence

\mathcal{V}(A_{N-1})
,
N=2,3,4,\dots
?
In other words, we ask whether
all VOAs
\mathcal{V}(A_{N-1})
can be recovered as
truncations of a single W-algebra,
which is expected to exist for generic values of the central charge and to admit an infinite
number of strong generators.
1
1

1

A subset
\mathcal{S}
of operators in a W-algebra is
a set of strong generators if
all operators in the W-algebra can be written as finite linear combinations of
normal ordered products of operators in
\mathcal{S}
and their derivatives.

Various examples of similar
universal W-algebras are available
in the literature. For instance, in the non-supersymmetric context,
one can consider the sequence of VOAs usually denoted

\mathcal{W}_{N}
,
N=2,3,4,\dots
,
where the case
N=2
is the Virasoro algebra, and
the case
N=3
is the

\mathcal{W}_{3}
algebra of Zamolodchikov

Zamolodchikov:1985wn
.
For each
N
,
\mathcal{W}_{N}
exists
for generic values of the central charge
c

and has finitely
many strong generators.
(The VOA
\mathcal{W}_{N}

is obtained by Drinfeld-Sokolov
reduction
Drinfeld:1984qv
;
Balog:1990dq
;
Bouwknegt:1992wg

of the
\mathfrak{su}(N)

affine Kac-Moody algebra.)
A universal W-algebra
for
\mathcal{W}_{N}
exists, usually denoted

\mathcal{W}_{\infty}[\mu]
,
discussed in

Figueroa-OFarrill:1992uuf
;
Khesin:1993ru
;
Khesin:1993ww

building on

Pope:1989ew
;
Bakas:1991fs
;
Bakas:1991gs
;
Yamagishi:1991ax
;
Figueroa-OFarrill:1991ynu
;
Yu:1991ng
;
Yu:1991bk
.
A mathematical proof of the existence
and properties of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}[\mu]
can be found in

Linshaw:2017tvv
.
The free parameters of

\mathcal{W}_{\infty}[\mu]
are
\mu
and the central charge
c
.
For generic values of
\mu
,

\mathcal{W}_{\infty}[\mu]
is a non-linear
2
2

2

Here non-linear refers to the fact that
the singular part of the OPE of
two strong generators contains
normal ordered products of
strong generators and their derivatives.

W-algebra with infinitely many strong generators.
In the limit
\mu\rightarrow N
,
N=2,3,4\dots
,

\mathcal{W}_{\infty}[\mu]
develops an ideal of null states; modding them out, it reduces to

\mathcal{W}_{N}
at central charge
c
.
The existence and properties
of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}[\mu]

are central to
the minimal model
holography program, see the review

Gaberdiel:2012uj

and references therein.
Even-spin version W-algebras
are also known
Gaberdiel:2011nt
;
Ahn:2011pv
;
Candu:2012ne
;
Kanade:2018qut
;
Prochazka:2019yrm
,
as well as
supersymmetric
versions

Creutzig:2011fe
;
Candu:2012jq
;
Candu:2012tr
;
Beccaria:2013wqa
;
Gaberdiel:2013vva
;

;

;
Gaberdiel:2014yla
;

;
Eberhardt:2018plx
;
Ahn:2020rev
;
Ahn:2022qex
;
Ahn:2022orj
;
Ahn:2023mdg
.

In this paper, we
conjecture that a universal W-algebra for
\mathcal{V}(A_{N-1})
exists,
which we denote
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

Leuv
.
We provide support
for this conjecture
based on
operator product expansion
(OPE)
bootstrap methods
(see e.g.
Blumenhagen:1990jv
).
We make extensive use
of the
Mathematica

package

OPEdefs

Thielemans:1991uw
;
Thielemans:1994er
.
Our proposed
universal W-algebra
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

has small
\mathcal{N}=4

supersymmetry
but differs from other models
in the literature
with the same supersymmetry

Ahn:2020rev

because of the quantum numbers
of its strong generators
(besides those of the
small
\mathcal{N}=4
super Virasoro algebra).
Furthermore, we find that
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

has no free parameters besides its central charge.
This sets it apart compared to other examples
mentioned in the previous paragraph.
Upon setting
c=-3(N^{2}-1)
to truncate

\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
to
\mathcal{V}(A_{N-1})
, no tunable parameter is left.
This is consistent with the expectation that

\mathcal{V}(A_{N-1})
for
N\geq 3

does not admit any continuous deformation,
and that its very existence relies on intricate patterns
of null states.

Note added.
While this paper was being finalized

Gaberdiel:2025eaf
appeared, which has some overlap
with this work.

Summary of results

The W-algebra
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

We propose that a non-linear W-algebra denoted
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
exists,
with the following properties:

(1)

\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
contains the small
\mathcal{N}=4
super Virasoro algebra as a subalgebra.

Thus, the operator content of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
is organized in multiplets of
the superconformal algebra
3
3

3

In this work, otherwise stated, we work with complexified Lie algebras.

\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
, the global part of the small
\mathcal{N}=4
super Virasoro algebra.
The bosonic subalgebra of

\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
is

\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{z}\oplus\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}
, where the first summand is the conformal algebra in two dimensions, and the second factor is an R-symmetry.

(2)

\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
is strongly generated by
the operators
collected in the following table.

\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
multiplets

\mathbb{J}

\mathbb{W}_{p}
(
p=3,4,5,\dots
)

The strong generators are organized in

short

\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
multiplets.
(The labels s,s in
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
stand for
“small” and “short.”)
The multiplet denoted
\mathbb{J}

collects the generators of the small
\mathcal{N}=4

super Virasoro algebra, with
T
the stress tensor,
J
the affine
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}
current,
and
G
,
\widetilde{G}
the supercurrents.
Besides
\mathbb{J}
,
we have one additional
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
multiplet
\mathbb{W}_{p}
for each
p=3,4,5,\dots
.

(3)

For each
p=3,4,5,\dots

the operator

W_{p}
is a Grassmann even super Virasoro primary operator.

(4)

The W-algebra
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
exists for generic values of the central charge
c
.

\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

has no free parameters besides
c
.

We argue for the existence and properties of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
by
bootstrapping
the OPEs of strong generators.
We leverage symmetry under the small
\mathcal{N}=4
super Virasoro algebra, and the assumption that
W_{p}
be a super Virasoro primary.
Thus, the non-trivial task is the determination of the OPEs
W_{p_{1}}\times W_{p_{2}}
.
We implement all these OPEs with

p_{1}+p_{2}\leq 10
.
Associativity allows us to fix all coefficients in the

W_{p_{1}}\times W_{p_{2}}
OPEs with

p_{1}+p_{2}\leq 9
;
for
p_{1}+p_{2}=10

we have partial results.
Our findings are summarized in Tables

1
-
9

and equation (
26
), where
we make use of a parameter
\nu
related to the central charge
c
by
c=3(1-\nu)
.

Next, we conjecture the following additional properties of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
.

(5)

If we tune the central charge to the values

c=-3(N^{2}-1)\ ,\qquad N=2,3,4,5,\dots,

(1)

\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
develops an ideal of null states.
In particular,
all generators in the multiplets

\mathbb{W}_{p}
with
p>N
are null.
The simple quotient of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
is
isomorphic to
the VOA

\mathcal{V}(A_{N-1})
associated to 4d
\mathcal{N}=4

\mathfrak{su}(N)

super Yang-Mills.
We stress that the ideal of null states
of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

contains states constructed with
\mathbb{W}_{p}
with
p\leq N
,
which are the VOA avatars of Higgs branch relations
of super Yang-Mills.

(6)

The W-algebra
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
admits an increasing weight based filtration
for generic values of the central charge.
The simple quotient of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
at
c=-3(N^{2}-1)
inherits the filtration, and thus per the conjecture above
gives a filtration on
\mathcal{V}(A_{N-1})
. The latter is identified with the
R
-filtration.

We recall that accessing the quantum number
R
in the VOA

\mathcal{V}(A_{N-1})
is essential to being able to
invert the map from 4d operators to 2d operators and therefore
extract valuable information about 4d physics from the
VOA.

We provide various pieces of evidence for the
connection between the W-algebra
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
and
4d
\mathcal{N}=4

\mathfrak{su}(N)
super Yang-Mills.
First, we perform a detailed counting of states
in
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
up to conformal weight
h=4
,
for various values of
N
, and check it against
predictions from the Macdonald index and Hall-Littlewood chiral ring of 4d
\mathcal{N}=4

\mathfrak{su}(N)
super Yang-Mills.
Second, we test the counting of states in
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
with the
BRST construction of
Beem:2013sza
of the VOA
\mathcal{V}(A_{N-1})
.
Finally, we also perform some checks against the
free field realization of
\mathcal{V}(A_{N-1})

Bonetti:2018fqz
;
Arakawa:2023cki
.
As a side product,
we exhibit the free-field realization
for
N=5
,
and the full set of OPE coefficients of

\mathcal{V}(A_{4})
,
which had not appeared in the literature before.
They are collected in
(
67
)
and Appendix
B
.

The W-algebra
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
and half-BPS correlators in 4d super Yang-Mills

The information contained in the W-algebra
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

can be used to compute
a protected part of
correlators of a class of 1/8-BPS operators
in 4d
\mathcal{N}=4

\mathfrak{su}(N)
super Yang-Mills,
for any
N
. More precisely, the operators in question
are the Schur operators, reviewed briefly in Section
4.1
.
The protected part of correlators of Schur operators
can in principle be computed in 4d by Wick contractions,
provided one knows the explicit forms of the operators.
The problem greatly simplifies for half-BPS operators, as
they do not participate in the mechanism of multiplet recombination.
Thus, the map between OPE data in
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
and
4d correlators is transparent
for half-BPS operators.

To formulate precisely the correspondence
between
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
and half-BPS operators
in 4d,
we use the notion of
single-particle operator

Aprile:2018efk
;
Aprile:2020uxk
. We recall that a basis of
the half-BPS chiral
ring of 4d
\mathcal{N}=4

SU(N)

super Yang-Mills is provided by single-trace
operators
T_{p}={\rm Tr}(\phi^{p})

and their products, the multi-trace operators.
(Here
\phi
denotes schematically the six real
adjoint-valued scalars of 4d
\mathcal{N}=4
super Yang-Mills.)
By definition, the single-particle operator

\mathcal{O}_{p}
is the combination

\mathcal{O}_{p}=T_{p}+\text{(multi-trace corrections)}

(2)

that has vanishing two-point
functions with
any
multi-trace operator.
Some explicit examples of operators

\mathcal{O}_{p}
can be found in (
5.1.1
).
The operator
\mathcal{O}_{p}
is identically zero for
p>N
.
(In contrast, for
p>N
the single-trace
operator
T_{p}
reduces to a complicated
combination of multi-trace operators via
trace relations.)

Now, the key observation is that
the two- and three-point
functions of half-BPS operators
are
in 4d
\mathcal{N}=4

SU(N)
super Yang-Mills
are
protected

Howe:1995aq
;
DHoker:1998vkc
;
Howe:1998zi
;
Intriligator:1998ig
;
Gonzalez-Rey:1999ouj
;
Intriligator:1999ff
;
Eden:1999gh
;
Skiba:1999im
;
Penati:1999ba
;
Dolan:2004mu

and can be computed in
the
free-field limit using Wick contractions.
We find it convenient to perform the rescaling

\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{p}=\frac{1}{N^{(p-2)/2}}\mathcal{O}_{p}\ .

(3)

Then, under the chiral algebra map of
Beem:2013sza
,
the operator
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{p}
maps to the strong generator
W_{p}
,

\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{p}\mapsto W_{p}\ .

(4)

We note that 2- and 3-point functions of

\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{p}
operators
are rational functions of
N^{2}

(and not merely rational functions of
N
).
We claim that, if we perform the
formal replacement

N^{2}\rightarrow 1-c/3
,
the
SU(N)
theory correlators

\langle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{p}\rangle
,

\langle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{p_{1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{p_{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{p_{3}}\rangle

yield a subset of OPE coefficients of the W-algebra

\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

as exact functions of
c
,

\begin{array}[]{c}\langle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{p}\rangle\ ,\ \langle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{p_{1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{p_{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{p_{3}}\rangle\\
\text{rational functions of $N^{2}$}\end{array}\;\;\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447ptN^{2}\leadsto 1-c/3\hskip 8.19447pt}\;\;\begin{array}[]{c}\text{W-algebra OPE coefficients}\\
g_{p}(c)\ ,\ c_{p_{1}p_{2}}{}^{p_{3}}(c)\end{array}\ .

(5)

Here
g_{p}
and
c_{p_{1}p_{2}}{}^{p_{3}}
are defined
by
W_{p}\times W_{p}\supset g_{p}\text{1}
,

W_{p_{1}}\times W_{p_{2}}\supset c_{p_{1}p_{2}}{}^{p_{3}}W_{p_{3}}
.
The precise formulation
of the correspondence
(
5
)
can be found in equations
(
144
),
(
145
), (
146
).
In stating
(
5
)
we are implicitly choosing
a normalization for the
W_{p}

generators on the W-algebra side. This is the normalization in which the results of equations
(
26
)
and Tables

2
-
9

are presented.

The wedge algebra of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

We complement our OPE bootstrap
analysis of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

with a study of its wedge algebra, which we denote

\text{Wedge}(\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}})
.
Recall that,
given a family of W-algebras
\mathcal{W}(c)
with central charge
c
,
its wedge algebra
\text{Wedge}(\mathcal{W}(c))

is a Lie algebra obtained
from
\mathcal{W}(c)
in two steps

Bowcock:1991zk

(see also
Gaberdiel:2011wb
for a review).
(i) For each strong generator
(say
X
)
of the W-algebra, expand

X
in Laurent modes as

X(z)=\sum_{m}z^{-m-h_{X}}

and retain only the “wedge modes”
|m|<h_{X}

(here
h_{X}
is the conformal
weight of
X
).
(ii) Consider the limit
of large central charge.
Heuristically, step (i)
eliminates all central terms in the W-algebra, and step (ii)
suppresses all non-linearities of
\mathcal{W}(c)
, to yield an ordinary
Lie algebra.

The wedge algebra

\text{Wedge}(\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}})

is an infinite-dimensional
higher-spin Lie superalgebra,
analogous
to the higher-spin algebra considered in the minimal model holography program
(see review
Gaberdiel:2012uj
).
It has been considered in

Costello:2018zrm
in the context
of twisted holography,
where it was denoted

\mathfrak{a}_{\infty}

and it was described as
the superalgebra of global
symmetries of the large-
N

VOA associated to
4d
\mathcal{N}=4

\mathfrak{su}(N)
super Yang-Mills.
In this work,
we recover this Lie superalgebra by means of a
direct Lie algebra bootstrap.
In the process, we identify closed forms for all its
(anti)commutators.
More precisely, we seek a Lie superalgebra containg

\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
,
whose generators
have the same quantum numbers
as the wedge modes of the strong generators of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
.
Interestingly, these assumptions
are enough to completely single out an infinite dimensional
Lie superalgebra which we denote

\mathfrak{hs}^{\text{s,s}}
. Here hs stands for higher spin.
(The wedge algebra of the W-algebra of
Ahn:2020rev

has the same supersymmetry as

\mathfrak{hs}^{\text{s,s}}
, but a different spectrum.)
The generators of

\mathfrak{hs}^{\text{s,s}}
are listed in
(
155
),
(
156
).
The (anti)commutators of

\mathfrak{hs}^{\text{s,s}}
are given in closed
form in Appendix

C
.
4
4

4

Even though we have closed expressions for all
(anti)commutators,
we have only
verified a finite subset of
Jacobi identities.

The Lie superalgebra

\mathfrak{hs}^{\text{s,s}}
has no free parameter.
Covariance under

\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)

restricts heavily
its structure;
the structure constants that are not fixed by
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
symmetry
turn out to have a particularly simple form, see
(
160
).

Having
independently
boostrapped
the Lie superalgebra
\mathfrak{hs}^{\text{s,s}}
,
we finally propose that indeed

\mathfrak{hs}^{\text{s,s}}\cong\text{Wedge}(\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}})\ .

(6)

We perform explicit tests of this proposal using the large central charge limit of the
OPE coefficients reported in Tables

2
-
9
.

2
Bootstrapping
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

In this section we
summarize the assumptions and strategy underlying
our bootstrap analysis of the W-algebra

\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
,
and we present its main results.
Our key findings are the OPEs
listed in Tables
1
to

9
.
Further details of the methodology used to derive the results of this
section are described in Appendix
A.2
.

2.1
Preliminary: small
\mathcal{N}=4
super Virasoro algebra

The bosonic
generators of the small
\mathcal{N}=4

super Virasoro algebra
are the stress tensor
T(z)

(of conformal weight 2)
and a triplet
J^{IJ}(z)=J^{JI}(z)
of affine

\mathfrak{sl}(2)
currents
(of weight 1).
The indices
I,J=1,2

are fundamental indices of

\mathfrak{sl}(2)
.
The fermionic generators
are
two doublets
G^{I}(z)
,
\widetilde{G}^{I}(z)

of weight 3/2.
We find it convenient to adopt a
standard
index-free notation based on the introduction of an auxiliary variable
y
.
More precisely, we define

\displaystyle J(z,y)=J^{IJ}(z)y_{I}y_{J}\ ,\quad G(z,y)=G^{I}(z)y_{I}\ ,\quad\widetilde{G}(z,y)=\widetilde{G}^{I}(z)y_{I}\ ,\quad y_{I}=(1,y)\ .

(7)

The
J\times J
and
T\times T

OPEs read

\displaystyle J(z_{1},y_{1})J(z_{2},y_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{-ky_{12}^{2}\text{1}}{z_{12}^{2}}+\frac{2y_{12}\big(1+\tfrac{1}{2}y_{12}\partial_{y_{2}}\big)J(z_{2},y_{2})}{z_{12}}+\text{reg.}\ ,

(8)

\displaystyle T(z_{1})T(z_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\tfrac{1}{2}c\text{1}}{z_{12}^{4}}+\frac{2T(z_{2})}{z_{12}^{2}}+\frac{\partial_{z_{2}}T(z_{2})}{z_{12}}+\text{reg.}\ .

We have introduced the notation

z_{12}=z_{1}-z_{2}
,

y_{12}=y_{1}-y_{2}
.
The central charge
c
is given in terms of the
affine Kac-Moody level
k

as

c=6k\ .

(9)

We record the full set
of OPEs of the small

\mathcal{N}=4
super Virasoro
algebra in Appendix
A.1
.

The small
\mathcal{N}=4

super Virasoro algebra
is invariant under an

SL(2)
outer automorphism
under which
T
,
J
are invariant and
G
,
\widetilde{G}
rotate as a doublet.
We denote the Cartan
generator of this

SL(2)
automorphism
as
\mathfrak{gl}(1)_{r}

and we assign charges
\pm\tfrac{1}{2}

to
G
,
\widetilde{G}

under
\mathfrak{gl}(1)_{r}
,
respectively.

The global part of the small

\mathcal{N}=4
super Virasoro algebra
is the superconformal
algebra

\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
.
Its bosonic subalgebra is

\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{z}\oplus\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}
,
where
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{z}
denotes the
global conformal algebra on the Riemann sphere
(associated to the coordinate
z
)
and
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}
is the R-symmetry
algebra (associated to the auxiliary
variable
y
).

In this work we encounter two
kinds of
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)

superconformal multiplets.
Firstly, we have short multiplets,
whose content is of the form

\text{short $\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)$ multiplet:}\qquad\begin{array}[]{ccc}&X\\
G_{X}:=G^{\downarrow}X&&\widetilde{G}_{X}:=\widetilde{G}^{\downarrow}X\\
&T_{X}:=\widetilde{G}^{\downarrow}G^{\downarrow}X\end{array}\ .

(10)

The operator
X
transforms
as a primary under

\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{z}\oplus\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}
.
It has
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}

spin
j\in\{1,\frac{3}{2},2,\frac{5}{2},\dots\}

and conformal weight
h=j
.
The notation
G^{\downarrow}X

denotes the
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{z}\oplus\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}

primary operator
of conformal weight

h+\frac{1}{2}
and
spin
j-\frac{1}{2}

that enters the order-one
pole in the singular OPE
of
G
with
X
.
The notation

\widetilde{G}^{\downarrow}

is defined similarly,
so that
\widetilde{G}^{\downarrow}X
is
an
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{z}\oplus\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}
primary
with conformal weight

h+\frac{1}{2}
and spin
j-\frac{1}{2}
. Finally,

\widetilde{G}^{\downarrow}G^{\downarrow}X
is
an
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{z}\oplus\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}
primary
with conformal weight

h+1
and spin
j-1
.
We refer to the operator
X

as the
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)

primary in the multiplet.

For example,
the generators

J
,
G
,
\widetilde{G}
,

T
of the
small
\mathcal{N}=4

super Virasoro algebra
form a short
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
multiplet,
which we denote

\mathbb{J}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}&J\\
G=G^{\downarrow}J&&\widetilde{G}=\widetilde{G}^{\downarrow}J\\
&T=\widetilde{G}^{\downarrow}G^{\downarrow}J\end{array}\right\}\ .

(11)

In this work we also
find
long
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
multiplets whose

\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
primary
has conformal weight
h

and spin
j
with
h<j
.
For generic
j
,
a long
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)

contains 16

\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{z}\oplus\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}
primary
operators, which are obtained
from the
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
primary by repeated application of OPE with the
fermionic currents
G
,
\widetilde{G}
. We refer the reader to Appendix
A.1

for further details.

We also need to recall the notion of super Virasoro
primary operator.
A super Virasoro primary operator
X
is a

\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)

primary operator satisfying an additional requirement:
in the OPEs of the generators

J
,
G
,
\widetilde{G}
,
T

of the super Virasoro
algebra with
X
, we encounter
no poles of order 2 or higher,
with the exception of the
standard order-2 pole in the
TX
OPE. We give
more details on super Virasoro
primary operators in Appendices

A.1
,
A.3
.

2.2
Assumptions and strategy of the bootstrap analysis

In this section we list the assumptions of our bootstrap
analysis and describe the strategy to implement it.

2.2.1
Assumptions

Our goal is to set up and perform a bootstrap
analysis to gather evidence for the existence
of a W-algebra
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
with the following
properties.

•

\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
contains the small

\mathcal{N}=4
super Virasoro algebra as a subalgebra.

•

The set of strong generators of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
consists of:

–

the generators
J
,
G
,
\widetilde{G}
,
T
of the small
\mathcal{N}=4
super-Virasoro algebra;

–

for each
p=3,4,5,\dots

one short
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
multiplet of operators

\mathbb{W}_{p}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}&W_{p}\\
G_{W_{p}}:=G^{\downarrow}W_{p}&&\widetilde{G}_{W_{p}}=\widetilde{G}^{\downarrow}W_{p}\\
&T_{W_{p}}:=\widetilde{G}^{\downarrow}G^{\downarrow}W_{p}\end{array}\right\}\ .

(12)

•

For each
p=3,4,5,\dots

the operator
W_{p}
is
a Grassmann-even super Virasoro primary with conformal weight and spin given as
h=j=\frac{1}{2}p
.

•

The central charge
c

of the small
\mathcal{N}=4
super Virasoro algebra is generic.
All operators
W_{p}
are non-null
operators.

The fact that
W_{p}
is not a null operator can be stated in terms of the two-point function
of
W_{p}
with itself.
By
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{z}\oplus\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}
symmetry,
this quantity takes the form

\langle W_{p}(z_{1},y_{1})W_{p}(z_{2},y_{2})\rangle=g_{p}\frac{y_{12}^{p}}{z_{12}^{p}}\ .

(13)

The two-point function coefficient
g_{p}
is assumed to be nonzero at generic values of the central charge
c
.

2.2.2
Strategy

Our goal is to fix the singular parts of the OPEs among the strong generators of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
. These OPEs are of three kinds,

\mathbb{J}\times\mathbb{J}\ ,\qquad\mathbb{J}\times\mathbb{W}_{p}\ ,\qquad\mathbb{W}_{p_{1}}\times\mathbb{W}_{p_{2}}\ .

(14)

We are using the following compact notation: if
\mathbb{A}
,

\mathbb{B}
denote

\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
multiplets,
then

\mathbb{A}\times\mathbb{B}

denotes collectively all OPEs
of the form

A\times B
,
where
A
is an
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{z}\oplus\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}

primary operator in the multiplet

\mathbb{A}
,
and
B
is an
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{z}\oplus\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}

primary operator in the multiplet
\mathbb{B}
.

The
\mathbb{J}\times\mathbb{J}
OPEs are the OPEs of generators
of the small
\mathcal{N}=4
super Virasoro algebra, reported in
(
168
).
The
\mathbb{J}\times\mathbb{W}_{p}
OPEs are completely fixed by
super Virasoro covariance
and by the assumption that

W_{p}
be a super Virasoro primary.
We report the

\mathbb{J}\times\mathbb{W}_{p}
OPEs in Appendix
A.1
.

Our main task is the determination of the OPEs
of the form

\mathbb{W}_{p_{1}}\times\mathbb{W}_{p_{2}}
.
We now describe our strategy to perform their computation.

Preliminary: OPE associativity

A key tool in our bootstrap analysis
is associativity of the OPE.
This can be characterized as follows.
Let
A(z)
,
B(z)
be two operators.
(Any dependence on the auxiliary variable
y
, if present, is suppressed for simplicity.)
The OPE
A\times B
can be parameterized as

A(z_{1})B(z_{2})=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{z_{12}^{n}}\{AB\}_{n}(z_{2})\ ,

(15)

where
z_{12}=z_{1}-z_{2}

and
\{AB\}_{n}(z_{2})
is identically
zero for sufficiently large
n
.
With this notation,
the associativity of the OPE
is encoded in the identities

borcherds1986vertex
;
kac1998vertex
;
Thielemans:1994er

\{A\{BC\}_{p}\}_{q}=(-1)^{|A||B|}\{B\{AC\}_{q}\}_{p}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\binom{q-1}{\ell-1}\{\{AB\}_{\ell}C\}_{p+q-\ell}\ ,\qquad p,q\in\mathbb{Z}\ .

(16)

Here
|A|=0,1
mod 2 is the Grassmann parity of the operator
A
.

The identity (
16
),
when
p
or
q
are non-positive,
encodes the rules to compute
OPEs of composite operators.
Throughout this work,
we assume that these rules hold.
We are then led to consider the identity
(
16
)
in the case in which
A
,
B
,
C

are strong generators of the algebra
and
p,q>0
.
Motivated by these considerations,
we introduce the following
notation.
Given operators
A
,
B
,
C
,
we define

\displaystyle\text{Jacobi}_{p,q}(A,B,C)

\displaystyle:=\{A\{BC\}_{p}\}_{q}-(-1)^{|A||B|}\{B\{AC\}_{q}\}_{p}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\binom{q-1}{\ell-1}\{\{AB\}_{\ell}C\}_{p+q-\ell}\ ,

(17)

\displaystyle\text{Jacobi}(A,B,C)

\displaystyle:=\left\{\text{Jacobi}_{p,q}(A,B,C)\ ,\ p,q\in\mathbb{N}\right\}\ .

If
\mathbb{A}
,
\mathbb{B}
,
\mathbb{C}
denote

\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
multiplets,
we also introduce the compact
notation

\text{Jacobi}(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{B},\mathbb{C})=\left\{\text{Jacobi}(A,B,C),A\in\mathbb{A},B\in\mathbb{B},C\in\mathbb{C}\right\}\ .

(18)

We note that

\text{Jacobi}(A,B,C)
is
implemented as the command

OPEJacobi[A,B,C]

in the
Mathematica

package
OPEdefs

Thielemans:1994er
.

In order to ensure the
associativity of the OPE,
we have to impose that
every element in the set

\text{Jacobi}(A,B,C)
is zero,
for any
A
,
B
,
C
strong generators.
Even though the set

\text{Jacobi}(A,B,C)
is indexed by
two positive integers
p
,
q
,
only a finite number of elements of

\text{Jacobi}(A,B,C)

can be potentially non-zero.
Therefore,
demanding that all elements of

\text{Jacobi}(A,B,C)
be zero
(for given
A
,
B
,
C
)
corresponds to a finite number of
constraints.
These are constraints on the OPE
coefficients in the singular parts of the OPEs among strong generators of the algebra.

In this paper we work
at generic values of the central charge
c
.
As a result, we expect that none
of the operators entering

\text{Jacobi}(A,B,C)
are null.
This simplifies our analysis:
we just have to set to zero
the coefficients of each
operator appearing in

\text{Jacobi}(A,B,C)
.

Workflow

The central task of the bootstrap
analysis is the determination of the

\mathbb{W}_{p_{1}}\times\mathbb{W}_{p_{2}}
OPEs. Even though we have an infinite number of multiplets

\mathbb{W}_{p}
, we can proceed
systematically by studying the

\mathbb{W}_{p_{1}}\times\mathbb{W}_{p_{2}}
OPEs for increasing values
of
p_{1}+p_{2}
,
starting from
p_{1}+p_{2}=6
.
Our strategy is summarized in the
flowchart in figure
1
.
In the following subsection
we describe how
the Ansatz for
\mathbb{W}_{p_{1}}\times\mathbb{W}_{p_{2}}
is constructed.
For the construction of composite
super Virasoro primary operators
we refer the reader to Appendix

A.5
.

Initialize
n=6
.

Construct the Ansätze
for the OPEs

\mathbb{W}_{p_{1}}\times\mathbb{W}_{p_{2}}
with

p_{1}+p_{2}=n
,
p_{1}\leq p_{2}
,

including
composite super Virasoro primaries
constructed so far.

Impose the constraints on OPE coefficients from

\text{Jacobi}(\mathbb{W}_{q_{1}},\mathbb{W}_{q_{2}},\mathbb{W}_{q_{3}})

with

q_{1}+q_{2}+q_{3}=n+2
.

Construct a basis of
composite super Virasoro primary

operators of weight

h=\frac{1}{2}n-\frac{1}{2}
.

Update
n
(
n\leftarrow n+1
).

Figure 1:
Flowchart describing our strategy to implement and constrain the OPEs

\mathbb{W}_{p_{1}}\times\mathbb{W}_{p_{2}}
proceeding by increasing values of
p_{1}+p_{2}
.

It can be useful to unpack
the flowchart of figure

1

and list explicitly
the steps
we have followed in implementing
our bootstrap analysis in this work:

1.

Construct the Ansatz for
\mathbb{W}_{3}\times\mathbb{W}_{3}
.

2.

Construct the Ansatz for
\mathbb{W}_{3}\times\mathbb{W}_{4}
.

3.

Impose the constraints on OPE coefficients from

\text{Jacobi}(\mathbb{W}_{3},\mathbb{W}_{3},\mathbb{W}_{3})
.

4.

Construct the composite super Virasoro primary operators

\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}
,

\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}
.

5.

Construct the Ansätze for
\mathbb{W}_{3}\times\mathbb{W}_{5}
,

\mathbb{W}_{4}\times\mathbb{W}_{4}
.

6.

Impose the constraints
on OPE coefficients
from

\text{Jacobi}(\mathbb{W}_{3},\mathbb{W}_{3},\mathbb{W}_{4})
.

7.

Construct the composite super Virasoro primary operators

\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{1}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}
,

\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{3}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}
,

\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{5}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}
,

\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{7}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}
.

8.

Construct the Ansätze for
\mathbb{W}_{3}\times\mathbb{W}_{6}
,

\mathbb{W}_{4}\times\mathbb{W}_{5}
.

9.

Impose the constraints
on OPE coefficients
from

\text{Jacobi}(\mathbb{W}_{3},\mathbb{W}_{3},\mathbb{W}_{5})
,

\text{Jacobi}(\mathbb{W}_{3},\mathbb{W}_{4},\mathbb{W}_{4})
.

10.

Construct the composite super Virasoro primary operators

\mathcal{C}_{4,0}^{W_{3}W_{3}}
,

\mathcal{C}_{4,0}^{W_{4}W_{4}}

\mathcal{C}_{4,1}^{W_{3}W_{5}}
,

\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{3}}
,

\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{5}}
,

\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{4}W_{4}}

\mathcal{C}_{4,3}^{W_{3}W_{5}}
,

\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{3}W_{5}}
,

\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{4}W_{4}}
.

11.

Construct the Ansätze for
\mathbb{W}_{3}\times\mathbb{W}_{7}
,

\mathbb{W}_{4}\times\mathbb{W}_{6}
,

\mathbb{W}_{5}\times\mathbb{W}_{5}
.

12.

Impose the constraints
on OPE coefficients
from

\text{Jacobi}(\mathbb{W}_{3},\mathbb{W}_{3},\mathbb{W}_{6})
,

\text{Jacobi}(\mathbb{W}_{3},\mathbb{W}_{4},\mathbb{W}_{5})
,

\text{Jacobi}(\mathbb{W}_{4},\mathbb{W}_{4},\mathbb{W}_{4})
.

Clearly, this algorithm can in principle
be continued indefinitely. Due to computational
limitations, in this work
we terminate the algorithm
at step 12. above.

2.2.3
Ansatz for the OPEs

Let us now describe the salient features of our Ansatz
for the
\mathbb{W}_{p_{1}}\times\mathbb{W}_{p_{2}}
OPEs.
Crucially,
they are constrained by super Virasoro covariance (see Appendix
A.3
).
Indeed,
all
\mathbb{W}_{p_{1}}\times\mathbb{W}_{p_{2}}
OPEs can be reconstructed by knowledge of the OPE
W_{p_{1}}\times W_{p_{2}}
.
Secondly,
the content of the RHS
of the
W_{p_{1}}\times W_{p_{2}}

OPE can be organized into
contributions of super Virasoro primaries
and their descendants.
The possible super Virasoro primaries
that we can encounter on the RHS
of
W_{p_{1}}\times W_{p_{2}}
are the identity operator

1
, the generators

W_{p}
, as well as composite super Virasoro primary
operators.

The core of the bootstrap
analysis performed in this work
is the determination of the

W_{p_{1}}\times W_{p_{2}}
OPEs
with

p_{1}\leq p_{2}
and

p_{1}+p_{2}\leq 10
.
In the remainder of this section
we present our results for
these OPEs.

It is convenient to
adopt a compact notation
in which, on the RHS of
W_{p_{1}}\times W_{p_{2}}
,
we only list the super Virasoro primary operators
that contribute to the singular
part of that OPE, together with
their OPE coefficients.
In particular, we do not show explicitly
any of their super Virasoro descendants
and we suppress
all
z
and
y
coordinates and derivatives.
This information can be unambiguously
reconstructed exploiting super Virasoro covariance,
as discussed in Appendix
A.3
.

The OPEs
W_{p_{1}}\times W_{p_{2}}

with
p_{1}\leq p_{2}

and
p_{1}+p_{2}\leq 10

are collected in Table

1
in the compact
notation described in the previous paragraph.
The notation
\mathcal{C}_{h,j}^{W_{p}W_{q}}

indicates a composite super Virasoro primary operator
with conformal weight
h

and spin
j
,
constructed starting from
a product of
W_{p}
and
W_{q}
.
More explicitly,
the composite operators entering
the OPEs above are of the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}

\displaystyle=(W_{3}W_{3})_{0}^{1}+\dots\ ,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\mathcal{C}_{4,1}^{W_{3}W_{5}}

\displaystyle=(W_{3}W_{5})_{0}^{1}+\dots\ ,

(19)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}

\displaystyle=(W_{3}W_{3})_{0}^{3}+\dots\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{3}}

\displaystyle=(W_{3}W_{3})_{-1}^{2}+\dots\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{1}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}

\displaystyle=(W_{3}W_{4})_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\dots

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{5}}

\displaystyle=(W_{3}W_{5})^{2}_{0}+\dots

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{3}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}

\displaystyle=(W_{3}W_{4})_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}}+\dots\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{4}W_{4}}

\displaystyle=(W_{4}W_{4})_{0}^{2}+\dots

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{5}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}

\displaystyle=(W_{3}W_{4})_{0}^{\frac{5}{2}}+\dots\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{4,3}^{W_{3}W_{5}}

\displaystyle=(W_{3}W_{5})_{0}^{3}+\dots\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{7}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}

\displaystyle=(W_{3}W_{4})_{0}^{\frac{7}{2}}+\dots\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{3}W_{5}}

\displaystyle=(W_{3}W_{5})_{0}^{4}+\dots\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{4,0}^{W_{3}W_{3}}

\displaystyle=(W_{3}W_{3})_{-1}^{0}+\dots

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{4}W_{4}}

\displaystyle=(W_{4}W_{4})_{0}^{4}+\dots

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{4,0}^{W_{4}W_{4}}

\displaystyle=(W_{4}W_{4})_{0}^{0}+\dots\ .

We are using the notation
(AB)^{j}_{n}

defined in Appendix
A.1
.
Schematically,
if
A
,
B

are
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{z}\oplus\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}
primary operators,
their OPE is organized into

\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{z}\oplus\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}
primary operators;

(AB)^{j}_{n}
is the
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{z}\oplus\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}
primary operator
that enters the
A\times B

OPE in the pole in
z_{12}
of order

n
(so values
n\leq 0
correspond
to regular terms in the OPE).
The omitted terms
in (
19
)
are additional terms required to
have super Virasoro primary
operators.
Further details on the composite operators
listed in
(
19
) are reported in
Appendix
A.5
.

We point out that the W-algebra also contains composite super Virasoro primary operators that are constructed with three or more
W_{p}
’s, for example
an operator of the form

\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{3}W_{3}}_{\frac{9}{2},\frac{9}{2}}=(W_{3}(W_{3}W_{3})^{3}_{0})^{3}_{0}+\dots\ .

(20)

The lightest such operators have weight
h=9/2
. They
first appear in OPEs
W_{q_{1}}\times W_{q_{2}}
with
q_{1}+q_{2}=11
,
but in this work
we study
q_{1}+q_{2}\leq 10
.

\displaystyle W_{3}\times W_{3}

\displaystyle=g_{3}\text{1}+c_{33}{}^{4}W_{4}\ ,

\displaystyle W_{3}\times W_{4}

\displaystyle=c_{34}{}^{3}W_{3}+c_{34}{}^{5}W_{5}\ ,

\displaystyle W_{3}\times W_{5}

\displaystyle=c_{35}{}^{4}W_{4}+c_{35}{}^{6}W_{6}+c_{35}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}+c_{35}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}\ ,

\displaystyle W_{4}\times W_{4}

\displaystyle=g_{4}\text{1}+c_{44}{}^{4}W_{4}+c_{44}{}^{6}W_{6}+c_{44}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}+c_{44}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}\ ,

\displaystyle W_{3}\times W_{6}

\displaystyle=c_{36}{}^{3}W_{3}+c_{36}{}^{5}W_{5}+c_{36}{}^{7}W_{7}

\displaystyle+c_{36}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{3}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}}\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{3}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}+c_{36}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{5}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}}\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{5}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}+c_{36}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{7}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}}\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{7}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}\ ,

\displaystyle W_{4}\times W_{5}

\displaystyle=c_{45}{}^{3}W_{3}+c_{45}{}^{5}W_{5}+c_{45}{}^{7}W_{7}

\displaystyle+c_{45}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{1}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}}\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{1}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}+c_{45}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{3}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}}\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{3}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}+c_{45}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{5}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}}\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{5}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}+c_{45}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{7}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}}\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{7}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}\ ,

\displaystyle W_{3}\times W_{7}

\displaystyle=c_{37}{}^{4}W_{4}+c_{37}{}^{6}W_{6}+c_{37}{}^{8}W_{8}+c_{37}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}

\displaystyle+c_{37}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{3}}+c_{37}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{5}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{5}}+c_{37}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{4}W_{4}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{4}W_{4}}

\displaystyle+c_{37}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,3}^{W_{3}W_{5}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,3}^{W_{3}W_{5}}+c_{37}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{3}W_{5}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{3}W_{5}}+c_{37}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{4}W_{4}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{4}W_{4}}\ ,

\displaystyle W_{4}\times W_{6}

\displaystyle=c_{46}{}^{4}W_{4}+c_{46}{}^{6}W_{6}+c_{46}{}^{8}W_{8}+c_{46}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}+c_{46}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}

\displaystyle+c_{46}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,1}^{W_{3}W_{5}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,1}^{W_{3}W_{5}}+c_{46}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{3}}+c_{46}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{5}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{5}}+c_{46}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{4}W_{4}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{4}W_{4}}

\displaystyle+c_{46}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,3}^{W_{3}W_{5}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,3}^{W_{3}W_{5}}+c_{46}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{3}W_{5}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{3}W_{5}}+c_{46}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{4}W_{4}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{4}W_{4}}\ ,

\displaystyle W_{5}\times W_{5}

\displaystyle=g_{5}\text{1}+c_{55}{}^{4}W_{4}+c_{55}{}^{6}W_{6}+c_{55}{}^{8}W_{8}+c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}+c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}

\displaystyle+c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,0}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,0}^{W_{3}W_{3}}+c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,0}^{W_{4}W_{4}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,0}^{W_{4}W_{4}}+c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{3}}+c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{5}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{5}}

\displaystyle+c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{4}W_{4}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{4}W_{4}}+c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{3}W_{5}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{3}W_{5}}+c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{4}W_{4}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{4}W_{4}}\ .

Table 1:
Ansatz for
W_{p_{1}}\times W_{p_{2}}
OPEs studied in this work. We adopt a schematic notation in which on the RHS of an OPE we list the super Virasoro primary operators that contribute to the singular part of the OPE with their OPE coefficients. We omit all super Virasoro descendants and suppress all
z
,
y
coordinates and derivatives.
The notation
\mathcal{C}_{h,j}^{W_{p}W_{q}}
stands for a composite
super Virasoro primary operator
of weight
h
and spin
j
constructed out of
W_{p}W_{q}
,
see (
19
) and Appendix

A.5
.
We group the OPEs based on increasing
p_{1}+p_{2}
.

2.3
Results of the bootstrap analysis

Next, we discuss our results for the OPE coefficients
entering Table
1
.
We find it convenient to write the central charge
c
and the level
k
in terms of a new parameter
\nu
as

c=6k=3(1-\nu)\ .

(21)

We were able to fix all OPE coefficients in the OPEs

W_{3}\times W_{3}
,

W_{3}\times W_{4}
,

W_{3}\times W_{5}
,

W_{4}\times W_{4}
,

W_{3}\times W_{6}
,
and

W_{4}\times W_{5}

in terms of
\nu

and of the two-point function
constants
g_{p}
.
In contrast,
the OPE coefficients in the
OPEs

W_{3}\times W_{7}
,

W_{4}\times W_{6}
, and

W_{5}\times W_{5}
are only partially fixed.

Based on our results,
our expectation is that
all OPE coefficients would be
progressively
fixed, if we pushed the bootstrap
analysis to
OPEs
W_{p_{1}}\times W_{p_{2}}

with higher and higher values of

p_{1}+p_{2}
.

To present explicitly the results of the bootstrap analysis, we have to make a choice of generators
W_{p}
. More precisely, we have to address two possible sources of ambiguity:

(i)

For
p\geq 6
, there exist composite super Virasoro primary operators constructed with
W_{q}
’s with
q<p
that have the same quantum numbers as
W_{p}
. As a result, we can always redefine
W_{p}
by shifting it by a linear combination of such composites.

(ii)

For
p\geq 3
the overall normalization of
W_{p}
is arbitrary.

The first examples of
shifts as in point (i) occur for
p=6,7
,

W_{6}^{\prime}=W_{6}+\mu_{6}\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{3}}_{3,3}\ ,\qquad W_{7}^{\prime}=W_{7}+\mu_{7}\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{4}}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{7}{2}}\ ,

(22)

where
\mu_{6}
,
\mu_{7}
are arbitrary constants.

To deal with (i) we propose the following general prescription:

\begin{array}[]{c}\text{$W_{p}$ must have vanishing two-point function with}\\
\text{any composite super Virasoro primary
built with $W_{q}$'s with $q<p$.}\end{array}

(23)

We expect that this prescription fixes any ambiguity of type (i).
We have checked this claim explicitly for
p=6,7
.

Next, we consider point (ii).
The freedom in the normalization of
W_{p}

implies that the two-point function coefficients
g_{p}
can be set to any desired value.
In this subsection we observe that a normalization choice exists in which the OPE coefficients of the W-algebra take a particularly simple form.
This choice is heavily inspired by half-BPS operators in 4d
SU(N)
super Yang-Mills,
and in particular by the notion
of single-particle operators
Aprile:2018efk
;
Aprile:2020uxk
.
The precise connection between the W-algebra and field theory
is discussed in Section

5
.

To describe our choice of normalization for
g_{p}
we need to introduce some notation.
For integer
p\geq 3
,
we define the following function of
\nu
,

S_{p}(\nu)=\frac{(\sqrt{\nu}+1)_{p-1}-(\sqrt{\nu}-p+1)_{p-1}}{p(p-1)}\frac{\nu^{\lfloor\frac{p-2}{2}\rfloor}}{\nu^{\frac{p-2}{2}}}\ .

(24)

In the above expression

(a)_{n}=\prod_{j=0}^{n-1}(a+j)

is the (ascending) Pochhammer symbol.
Even if it is not apparent
from (
24
),

S_{p}(\nu)
is a monic polynomial in
\nu

of degree
\lfloor\frac{p-2}{2}\rfloor

with integer coefficients.
The explicit expressions of
S_{p}(\nu)
for the first few values of
p
are

\displaystyle S_{3}(\nu)

\displaystyle=1\ ,

(25)

\displaystyle S_{4}(\nu)

\displaystyle=\nu+1\ ,

\displaystyle S_{5}(\nu)

\displaystyle=\nu+5\ ,

\displaystyle S_{6}(\nu)

\displaystyle=\nu^{2}+15\nu+8\ ,

\displaystyle S_{7}(\nu)

\displaystyle=\nu^{2}+35\nu+84\ ,

\displaystyle S_{8}(\nu)

\displaystyle=\nu^{3}+70\nu^{2}+469\nu+180\ ,

\displaystyle S_{9}(\nu)

\displaystyle=\nu^{3}+126\nu^{2}+1869\nu+3044\ .

Our choice of normalization
for
W_{p}
(up to a sign)
can then be stated as
declaring the two-point function coefficient
g_{p}
to be given by

g_{p}=p\nu\frac{\prod_{r=1}^{p-1}(\nu-r^{2})}{S_{p}(\nu)}\frac{\nu^{\lfloor\frac{p-2}{2}\rfloor}}{\nu^{p-1}}\ .

(26)

This is a rational function of
\nu
.

We are now in a position to list the OPE coefficients extracted
from the bootstrap analysis
in the normalization
(
26
).
This is done in Tables

2
-
9
.
We refer the reader to Appendix

A.2.4
for the same results, in which the values of the two-point function coefficients
g_{p}

are left arbitrary.

Some comments are in order:

•

All OPE coefficients are rational functions of
\nu
.

•

In writing the OPE coefficients we have implicitly fixed some sign ambiguities in the generators
W_{p}
. (Clearly
setting (
26
)
still allows for a sign flip

W_{p}\mapsto-W_{p}
.)

•

The bootstrap analysis does not fix the OPE coefficients
c_{36}{}^{3}

and
c_{37}{}^{4}
.
This is expected because of ambiguities
of the form (
22
) in the definition of
W_{6}
,
W_{7}
.
Here we have fixed these OPE coefficients to zero,

c_{36}{}^{3}=0\ ,\qquad c_{37}{}^{4}=0\ .

(27)

Indeed,
this is equivalent to imposing
orthogonality of
W_{6}
,
W_{7}

with composite super Virasoro primary operators,

\langle W_{6}\;\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{3}}_{3,3}\rangle=0\ ,\qquad\langle W_{7}\;\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{4}}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{7}{2}}\rangle=0\ .

(28)

in accordance with the general prescription (
23
).

•

We observe that
some OPE coefficients are
\nu
-independent. In particular,

c_{q_{1}q_{2}}{}^{p}=q_{1}q_{2}\ ,\qquad\text{for $p\geq q_{1}$, $p\geq q_{2}$, $q_{1}+q_{2}=p+2$}\ .

(29)

At this stage, we can regard this as an empirical observation about the results of the bootstrap analysis. In Section

5
we will relate
(
29
)
to correlators of half-BPS operators in 4d
\mathcal{N}=4

SU(N)
super Yang-Mills.

•

We can interpret (
29
) as follows.
Let us order the OPEs

W_{q_{1}}\times W_{q_{2}}

for increasing values of
q_{1}+q_{2}
. The generator

W_{p}
appears for the first time in the OPEs for
q_{1}+q_{2}=p+2
.
Thus, we can
define

W_{p}
to be the super Virasoro primary operator with
h=j=p/2
that enters
W_{q_{1}}\times W_{q_{2}}
with the fixed coefficient
q_{1}q_{2}
and that satisfies
(
23
).

If there are several pairs
(q_{1},q_{2})
with
q_{1}+q_{2}=p+2
, it does not matter which one we pick to define
W_{p}
. This is a non-trivial feature of the W-algebra.

•

The values of
c_{37}{}^{8}
,
c_{46}{}^{8}
,
c_{55}{}^{8}
reported above are educated guesses, based on
(
29
). Our bootstrap analysis is not powerful enough to fix them independently. It provides the relations

c_{46}{}^{8}=\frac{8}{7}c_{37}{}^{8}\ ,\qquad c_{55}{}^{8}=\frac{25}{21}c_{37}{}^{8}\ .

(30)

These are indeed compatible with our educated guesses.

•

The OPE coefficients
c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{5}}_{4,4}}
,

c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}^{W_{4}W_{4}}_{4,4}}
are not completely fixed at this
stage of the bootstrap, but we do expect that they would be fixed if we pushed the bootstrap analysis to higher weights.

•

The two-point factors
g_{p}
as given by
(
26
) are zero for special values of
\nu
. This will be important when we discuss some truncations
of the W-algebra
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
in Section
3
.

•

We observe that, in the limit of large
\nu
, we have

\nu\rightarrow\infty\;:\qquad g_{p}=p\nu+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{0})\ ,\qquad c_{q_{1}q_{2}}{}^{p}=q_{1}q_{2}+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{-1})\ .

(31)

Crucially, the second relation holds (conjecturally)
for
any

q_{1}
,
q_{2}
,
p
in the large
\nu
limit.
It is straightforward to check that it is satisfied for all
the OPE coefficients reported in Tables
2
-
9
.
Further insights into
(
31
)
are provided by the connection
with correlators of half-BPS operators explained in
Section
5
.

The observations
(
31
) will be important in Section
6
for our discussion of the wedge algebra
of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
.

OPE coefficients in
W_{3}\times W_{3}
and

W_{3}\times W_{4}

\displaystyle c_{33}{}^{4}

\displaystyle=9\ ,

\displaystyle c_{34}{}^{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{12(\nu-9)}{\nu+1}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{34}{}^{5}

\displaystyle=12\ .

2.4
Filtration on
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

In this subsection we discuss a
filtration structure on
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
,
which we refer to as

\mathfrak{R}
-filtration.
Evidence for this filtration comes
from the
OPE coefficients reported in Tables
2
-
9
.
The
\mathfrak{R}
-filtration is a weight-based increasing
filtration.
More explicitly, we assign weights to the strong generators
of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
, according to the following table,

\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
multiplets

\mathbb{J}

\mathbb{W}_{p}
(
p=3,4,5,\dots
)

operators

J

G

\widetilde{G}

T

W_{p}

G_{W_{p}}

\widetilde{G}_{W_{p}}

T_{W_{p}}

\mathfrak{R}
-weight

1

1

1

1

\frac{p}{2}

\frac{p}{2}

\frac{p}{2}

\frac{p}{2}

The identity operator
1
and the
derivative
\partial_{z}
are assigned weight zero.
The weight of a normal ordered product is the sum of the weights
of the operators that enter it.
The weight of a linear combination of terms is
the maximum of the weights of each summand.
We define

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{h,j,r}

\displaystyle=\{\text{$\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}$ primaries of definite $(h,j,r)$}\}\ ,

(32)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{h,j,r,\mathfrak{R}}

\displaystyle=\{\text{$\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}$ primaries of definite $(h,j,r)$ and weight $\mathfrak{R}-k$, $k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}$}\}\ .

(The states on the RHSs of the above relations must have definite
h
, but need not
be
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{z}
primaries.)
By construction, summing
\mathfrak{F}_{h,j,r,\mathfrak{R}}

over
\mathfrak{R}\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}

reproduces the full space

\mathcal{V}_{h,j,r}
.
The associated graded is

\mathrm{gr}\mathcal{V}_{h,j,r}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{R}\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathfrak{G}_{h,j,r,\mathfrak{R}}\ ,\qquad\mathfrak{G}_{h,j,r,\mathfrak{R}}=\mathfrak{F}_{h,j,r,\mathfrak{R}}/\mathfrak{F}_{h,j,r,\mathfrak{R}-1}\ .

(33)

We also claim that the
\mathfrak{R}
-filtration of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

satisfies the following additional properties.
Suppose
\mathcal{O}_{1}\in\mathfrak{F}_{h_{1},j_{1},r_{1},\mathfrak{R}_{1}}

and
\mathcal{O}_{2}\in\mathfrak{F}_{h_{2},j_{2},r_{2},\mathfrak{R}_{2}}
.
Parametrize the full

\mathcal{O}_{1}\times\mathcal{O}_{2}
OPE
as

\mathcal{O}_{1}(z_{1},y_{1})\mathcal{O}_{2}(z_{2},y_{2})=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{j}\frac{y_{12}^{j_{1}+j_{2}-j}}{z_{12}^{n}}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{j_{1},j_{2};j}(y_{12},\partial_{y_{2}})\{\mathcal{O}_{1}\mathcal{O}_{2}\}^{j}_{n}(z_{2},y_{2})\ .

(34)

Here
z_{12}=z_{1}-z_{2}
,

y_{12}=y_{1}=y_{2}
. On the RHS of the above expression we have decomposed
the resultin irreducible representations of
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}
.
The sums over
j
is over the usual spins
allowed by
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}
selection rules
when combining an irreducible representation of spin
j_{1}
and one of spin
j_{2}
.
The quantity
\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{j_{1},j_{2};j}(y_{12},\partial_{y_{2}})

is a differential operator, see (
176
)
for its expression.
The operator
\{\mathcal{O}_{1}\mathcal{O}_{2}\}^{j}_{n}(z_{2},y_{2})
is by construction
an
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}
primary, but needs not be
an
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{z}
primary.
With this notation, our claim about the

\mathfrak{R}
filtration is as follows,

\displaystyle\{\mathcal{O}_{1}\mathcal{O}_{2}\}^{j}_{0}

\displaystyle\in\mathfrak{F}_{h_{1}+h_{2},j,r_{1}+r_{2},\mathfrak{R}_{1}+\mathfrak{R}_{2}}

\displaystyle\text{(normal ordered product)}\ ,

(35)

\displaystyle\{\mathcal{O}_{1}\mathcal{O}_{2}\}^{j}_{1}

\displaystyle\in\mathfrak{F}_{h_{1}+h_{2}-1,j,r_{1}+r_{2},\mathfrak{R}_{1}+\mathfrak{R}_{2}-1}

\displaystyle\text{(simple pole in the OPE)}\ .

While we do not have a proof of the above claims,
we have verified that they are compatible with the
explicit OPE data that we were able to fix
with the bootstrap analysis.

Another property of the
\mathfrak{R}

filtration is that all states in the same

\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
multiplet
have the same
\mathfrak{R}
degree.
This is apparent for the strong generators
listed in the table above,
and it can also be checked in some examples of
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
multiplets of low conformal weight.

Tuning the central charge and simple quotients.

The
\mathfrak{R}
-filtration described above is defined for generic values of the central charge
c
.
We may also consider the case in which
the central charge is tuned to a special value,
and
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
develops an ideal of null states.
In this case, the
\mathfrak{R}
-filtration descends
to the simple quotient.
Let us define

\mathcal{N}_{h,j,r}=\{\text{null states in $\mathcal{V}_{h,j,r}$}\}\ .

(36)

The quotient by null states is a map

\pi:\mathcal{V}_{h,j,r}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{h,j,r}:=\mathcal{V}_{h,j,r}/\mathcal{N}_{h,j,r}\ ,\qquad\pi:v\mapsto v+\mathcal{N}_{h,j,r}\ .

(37)

The induced filtration is

\widetilde{\mathfrak{F}}_{h,j,r,\mathfrak{R}}=\pi\Big({\mathfrak{F}}_{h,j,r,\mathfrak{R}}\Big)\ .

(38)

In simpler terms,
to determine the weight of a state in the quotient,
we add arbitrary null states
and tune them to minimize the weight.

3
Truncation: from
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
to
\mathcal{V}(A_{N-1})

The bootstrap analysis of the previous section provides strong
evidence for the existence of a
W-algebra
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
whose only free parameter
5
5

5

Up to the choice of normalization of the generators and the freedom discussed around (
22
)
is the central charge
c
.
In this section we study

\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

as the central charge approaches the values
6
6

6

One could ask the converse question namely for which value of the central charge
c
all the strong generators in the multiplets
\mathbb{W}_{p}
with
p>N
are nulls.
While we do not have a complete answer to this question, we know that there are more values compared to the one given in (
1
), for example
c=3
for
\mathcal{W}(2,3)

Bonetti:2018fqz
.

c=-3(N^{2}-1)\quad\text{or equivalently}\quad\nu=N^{2}\ ,\qquad N=2,3,4,\dots\ .

(39)

For
\nu=N^{2}
,
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
develops a large ideal of null states,
which we denote
\mathcal{I}_{N}
. Recall that an ideal
\mathcal{I}

of a vertex algebra
\mathcal{V}
is a vector subspace of states satisfying the requirements

\partial^{k}A\in\mathcal{I}\ ,\quad\{AX\}_{n}\in\mathcal{I}\ ,\quad\{XA\}_{n}\in\mathcal{I}\ ,\quad\text{for any $A\in\mathcal{I}$, $X\in\mathcal{V}$, $n\in\mathbb{Z}$,
$k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}$}\ .

(40)

More precisely, we conjecture that
\mathcal{I}_{N}
satisfies the following:

•

\mathcal{I}_{N}
contains all strong generators
W_{p}
with
p>N
.

•

In fact,
\mathcal{I}_{N}
is generated by
W_{p}
with
p>N
.

•

The ideals
\mathcal{I}_{N}
satisfy strict inclusions as vector spaces
7
7

7

We refer the reader to
Chang:2024zqi
for a related discussion associated to the space of BPS operators in 4d SCFTs.

\mathcal{I}_{N}\supsetneq\mathcal{I}_{N+1}\ .

(41)

Clearly, the ideal generated by
W_{p>N}

contains all states written as normal ordered product
of some (derivative of)
W_{p}
with
p>N

with any other (derivative of a) strong generator.
More interestingly, the ideal generated by

W_{p>N}
also contains states that are found in the
singular part of the OPE of some
W_{p}
with
p>N
with any other
states. For example, by looking at the right hand side of the OPEs
W_{q}\times W_{p>N}
when
c=-3(N^{2}-1)
we discover additional null states that are necessary for the existence of the truncated W-algebra. These null states are associated with VOA avatars of Higgs branch relations and can be matched against the free field realization of
Bonetti:2018fqz
.

The choice of generators
W_{p}
(and their normalizations)
encoded in (
23
) and (
26
)
is particularly well-suited to analyze the limit
\nu\rightarrow N^{2}
for
N=2,3,\dots
.
Indeed, we observe that,
in this limit
all two-point function coefficients
g_{p}

in (
26
) and all
OPE coefficients
in Tables

2
-
9

remain finite.
8
8

8

For each
p\geq 4
, the roots of the polynomial
S_{p}(\nu)
are all
negative real numbers.
(This is a conjectural statement, which we have verified explicitly for
p\leq 60
.)

Moreover, it is clear from the expression
(
26
)
that
g_{p}
goes to zero as
\nu\rightarrow N^{2}
for
p>N
.

Besides the conjectures listed above, we also claim that

•

The simple quotient of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
at
\nu=N^{2}
by the ideal
\mathcal{I}_{N}
is a VOA
with small
\mathcal{N}=4
supersymmetry and strong generators

\mathbb{J}
,
\{\mathbb{W}_{p}\}_{p=3}^{N}
.
This VOA is isomorphic to
the VOA

\mathcal{V}(A_{N-1})

associated to the Coxeter group

A_{N-1}=\text{Weyl}(\mathfrak{a}_{N-1})=\text{Weyl}(\mathfrak{su}(N))
according to the proposal
of
Bonetti:2018fqz
, proven in
Arakawa:2023cki
.
It is also isomorphic to the VOA associated to
4d
\mathcal{N}=4

SU(N)
super Yang-Mills by the map of

Beem:2013sza
.

While we do not have rigorous proofs
of above claims,
we have gathered evidence in their
favor by studying some explicit examples.
In particular, in Section
3.1

we study some implication of the
W_{q}\times W_{p>N}
OPEs
for the structure of the ideal
\mathcal{I}_{N}
.
In Section
3.2
we analyze
explicitly the cases

N=2,3,4,5
, and compare
with the
VOA
\mathcal{V}(A_{N-1})

and its the free-field realization
proposed in
Bonetti:2018fqz

and proven in
Arakawa:2023cki
.

Remarks on inclusion.

The conjectural inclusion
(
41
) amounts to the statement that,
if a state is null for
\nu=N^{2}
, then
it is also null for
\nu=M^{2}
with
M=2,\dots,N-1
.
This is clear for the strong generators

W_{p}
, since
\mathcal{I}_{N}
is (conjecturally) generated by
W_{p>N}
.
This property, however, is less obvious for
states that are written as linear combinations of
normal ordered products of several strong generators and their derivatives. Nonetheless, we can verify that
(
41
) holds for all states in
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)

multiplets with
h\leq 4
, by means of explicit
computation of their norms.
We refer the reader to Appendix
D

for further details.

As an example of a nontrivial check of (
41
), let us discuss here the two-point function coefficients of the
composite super Virasoro primaries
listed in (
19
),
which can be computed
using their explicit expressions in Appendix
A.5
.
If we make use of (
26
) for the two-point functions
coefficients
g_{p}
, the results are as follows:

\displaystyle(h,j)

\displaystyle=(3,1)\ ,

\displaystyle\frac{480(\nu-16)(\nu-9)^{2}(\nu-4)^{3}(\nu-1)^{4}S_{8}(\nu)}{\nu^{5}(\nu+5)(\nu+9)(\nu+11)^{2}}\ ,

In the above list,
when there is a single composite super Virasoro primary operator, we have given its two-point function coefficients.
If there are two or more operators,
we have written the determinant of the matrix of
two-point function coefficients,
because the expressions of the full matrices are
quite cumbersome (the interested reader can find them in Appendix
A.5
).

3.1
Null states generated from the
W_{q}\times W_{p>N}
OPEs

We will now look more closely at the OPEs
W_{q}\times W_{p>N}
when
\nu=N^{2}
. The first interesting example is given by the OPE
W_{3}\times W_{N+1}
.
To analyze this case, let us first collect from Tables

2
-
9
the general form of the OPE

W_{3}\times W_{p+1}=g_{3}\,\delta_{2,p}\,\mathbf{1}+\,c_{3,p+1}{}^{p}\,W_{p}+\,3(p+1)\,W_{p+2}+\\
\mu_{p}^{+}\,\mathcal{C}_{\frac{p}{2}+1,\frac{p}{2}+1}+\mu_{p}^{0}\,\mathcal{C}_{\frac{p}{2}+1,\frac{p}{2}}+\mu_{p}^{-}\,\mathcal{C}_{\frac{p}{2}+1,\frac{p}{2}-1}\,.

(43)

In this expression
\mathcal{C}_{(h,j)}
denote composite super-Virasoro primaries and the OPE coefficients are given by

c_{3,p+1}{}^{p}(\nu)=3(p+1)(\nu-p^{2})\,\frac{S_{p}(\nu)}{S_{p+1}(\nu)}\frac{1}{\nu^{1-(p\,\text{mod}2)}}\,,

(44)

and

\big(\mu^{+}_{4}(\nu),\mu^{+}_{5}(\nu),\dots\big)=\big(\tfrac{15(\nu-16)(\nu-9)(\nu+3)}{\nu\,S_{5}(\nu)S_{6}(\nu)},\tfrac{36(\nu-25)(\nu-16)(\nu+7)}{S_{6}(\nu)S_{7}(\nu)},\dots\big)\,,

(45)

\big(\mu^{0}_{5}(\nu),\mu^{0}_{6}(\nu),\dots\big)=\big(\tfrac{3(\nu-25)(\nu-16)}{\nu\,S_{6}(\nu)},\tfrac{28(\nu-36)(\nu-24)}{5\,\nu\,S_{7}(\nu)},\dots\big)\,,

(46)

\big(\mu^{-}_{4}(\nu),\mu^{-}_{5}(\nu),\dots\big)=\big(\tfrac{-10(\nu-2)}{\nu\,S_{5}(\nu)},\tfrac{-21(\nu-1)}{S_{6}(\nu)},\dots\big)\,,

(47)

with
S_{p}(\nu)
is defined in (
24
). For low values of
p
some of the composites super-Virasoro primaries
\mathcal{C}
in (
43
) are absent, so we omit the corresponding
\mu
coefficient.
We recall that, as above, we organized the right and side of the OPEs in super-Virasoso families. In particular, when
p=2
the term
W_{2}
is absent and is a descendant of the identity
\mathbf{1}
.

When
\nu=N^{2}
, the operators
W_{p>N}
are null. So, when we quotient by these nulls, we should set to zero the result of the OPE of these nulls with any other operator. The simplest case is given by
W_{3}\times W_{N+1}
, which is a specialization of (
43
) to
p=N
and
\nu=N^{2}
. We observe that in this case
c_{3,N+1}{}^{N}(N^{2})=0
. This is good news, since the generator
W_{N}
is non zero. The generator
W_{N+2}
on the other hand appears with non vanishing OPE coefficient, but this is not a problem since we set this generator to zero in the quotient.
The cases
N=2
and
N=3
should be treated with care since in these cases the OPE coefficient of the super-Virasoro descendants of the identity and
W_{3}
respectively have poles when
\nu
approaches
4
and
9
. These poles cancel against the zero of
g_{3}
and
c_{34}^{3}
respectively to leave the operators

\mathcal{N}_{(2,0)}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}=(JJ)^{0}_{0}+\tfrac{1}{3}T\,,\qquad\mathcal{N}_{(\frac{5}{2},\frac{1}{2})}^{\mathfrak{su}(3)}=(JW_{3})^{\frac{1}{2}}_{0}+\tfrac{1}{4}T_{W_{3}}

(48)

This implies that these operators, whose norm is easily seen to vanish for
\nu=4
and
\nu=9
respectively, must be set to zero in the respective quotients.

For
N\geq 4
the super-Virasoro primaries
\mathcal{C}
contributes to the OPEs
W_{3}\times W_{N+1}
as well.
The OPE coefficients
\mu^{+}_{N}(N^{2})
,
\mu^{0}_{N}(N^{2})
vanish while
\mu^{-}_{N}(N^{2})\neq 0
. This implies that the operator that multiplies
\mu^{-}_{N}(N^{2})\neq 0
, namely

\mathcal{N}_{(\frac{N}{2}+1,\frac{N}{2}-1)}^{\mathfrak{su}(N)}=\mathcal{C}_{(\frac{N}{2}+1,\frac{N}{2}-1)}\Big|_{\nu=N^{2},W_{p>N}=0}\,,

(49)

must be set to zero in the quotient.
Also in this case, we can verify that the
operator (
50
) has zero norm,
see (
42
).
The null operator
\mathcal{N}_{(\frac{N}{2}+1,\frac{N}{2}-1)}^{\mathfrak{su}(N)}
given in (
48
) for
N=2,3
and in (
50
) for
N\geq 4
is the VOA avatar of the lightest Higgs branch relations.
We further elaborate of the interpretation of the nulls states from the four dimensional perspective in Section
4
.

For
N=2
the state
\mathcal{N}_{(2,0)}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}
generates the maximal ideal (after we have set
W_{p\geq 3}
to zero).
For higher values of
N
there are more generators of the maximal ideal.
To discover them, lets consider the OPE
W_{3}\times W_{N+2}
.
Looking at (
43
) with
p=N+1
and
\nu=N^{2}
we see that on the right hand side
W_{N+1}
and
W_{N+2}
are zero.
The OPE coefficients
\mu^{+}_{N+1}(N^{2})
,
\mu^{0}_{N+1}(N^{2})
vanish while
\mu^{-}_{N+1}(N^{2})\neq 0
. This implies that the operator that multiplies
\mu^{-}_{N+1}(N^{2})\neq 0
, namely

\mathcal{N}_{(\frac{N+3}{2},\frac{N+1}{2})}^{\mathfrak{su}(N)}=\mathcal{C}_{(\frac{N+3}{2},\frac{N+1}{2})}\Big|_{\nu=N^{2},W_{p>N}=0}\,,

(50)

must be set to zero in the quotient.
Also in this case, we can verify that the
operator (
50
) has zero norm.
As conjectured in
Bonetti:2018fqz
for
N=3
the states

\mathcal{N}_{(\frac{5}{2},\frac{1}{2})}^{\mathfrak{su}(3)}
and
\mathcal{N}_{(3,1)}^{\mathfrak{su}(3)}
generate the maximal ideal, but for
N\geq 4
we need more.
For low values of
N
we conjecture that the following nulls generate the maximal ideal

\displaystyle N=2:

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{(2,0)}

(51)

\displaystyle N=3:

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{(\frac{5}{2},\frac{1}{2})};\,\,\,\mathcal{N}_{(3,1)}

\displaystyle N=4:

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{(3,1)}\,;\,\,\,\mathcal{N}_{(\frac{7}{2},\frac{3}{2})},\,\,\mathcal{N}_{(\frac{7}{2},\frac{1}{2})};\,\,\,\mathcal{N}_{(4,2)},\,\mathcal{N}_{(4,0)}

\displaystyle N=5:

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{(\frac{7}{2},\frac{3}{2})};\,\,\,\mathcal{N}_{(4,2)},\,\mathcal{N}_{(4,1)},\,\mathcal{N}_{(4,0)};\,\,\,\mathcal{N}_{(\frac{9}{2},\frac{5}{2})},\,\,\mathcal{N}_{(\frac{9}{2},\frac{3}{2})},\,\,\mathcal{N}_{(\frac{9}{2},\frac{1}{2})}

We have already showed that the nulls in the first two columns are obtained by considering the OPEs
W_{3}\times W_{N+1}
and
W_{3}\times W_{N+2}
.
The OPE
W_{4}\times W_{N+1}
for example gives the second and third column of nulls and so on.

3.2
Analysis for
N=2,3,4,5

3.2.1
The case
N=2

The expression (
26
) for
g_{p}

shows that for
\nu\rightarrow 4

all
generators

W_{p}
become null operators.
This can also be argued differently as follows.

The W-algebra
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
contains the small
\mathcal{N}=4
super Virasoro algebra as a subalgebra. If we tune
\nu=4
, equivalently
c=-9
,
there is a non-trivial super Virasoro descendant of the identity operator that becomes super Virasoro primary
(hence, null).
In turn, this restricts the allowed
representations of the super Virasoro algebra.

More explicitly,
the following operator becomes super Virasoro primary,

\mathcal{N}=(JJ)^{0}_{0}+\frac{1}{3}T\ .

(52)

We now consider the OPE
\mathcal{N}\times W_{p}
. This is completely fixed by
the super Virasoro algebra and the assumption that
W_{p}
be a super Virasoro primary. Since
\mathcal{N}
is a null state, all operators appearing on the
RHS of the OPE
\mathcal{N}\times W_{p}
must be null. However, the operator
W_{p}
itself
appears on the RHS of this OPE, with coefficient

\mathcal{N}\times W_{p}\supset-\frac{p(p+1)}{6}W_{p}\ .

(53)

We see that, if we assume that
W_{p}

is not null, we get a contradiction.
Hence we conclude that
W_{p}
must be null.

After modding out by null states,
the W-algebra
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

reduces to a copy
of the
small
\mathcal{N}=4
superconformal algebra.
This indeed agrees with the VOA

\mathcal{V}(A_{1})
, which is the same as the
small
\mathcal{N}=4
superconformal algebra
at central charge
c=-9
,
see also
Adamovic:2014lra
.

3.2.2
The case
N=3

It is straightforward to check that all OPE coefficient in Tables
2
-
9

remain finite in the limit
\nu\rightarrow 9
. Moreover, we see from
(
26
) that the operators
W_{4}
,
W_{5}
, etc. become null.

Let us present an argument
for the fact that
W_{4}
must be null, which is independent on a choice of normalization for the
W_{p}
generators.
The norm squared of the composite super Virasoro primary operator

\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{3}}_{3,1}

is a function of
g_{3}
and
\nu
, given by (
268
), repeated here for convenience,
9
9

9

We use
\langle\mathcal{O}\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\rangle

to denote the coefficient of the identity operator

1
in the OPE
\mathcal{O}\times\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
.

\langle\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{3}}_{3,1}\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{3}}_{3,1}\rangle=\frac{9(\nu-16)(\nu-9)\nu}{5(\nu-11)(\nu-4)(\nu-2)}g_{3}^{2}\ .

(54)

We observe that
\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{3}}_{3,1}
becomes null
for
\nu=9
, even if
W_{3}
is not null (
g_{3}\neq 0
).
Moreover, we can compute the
OPE of
\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{3}}_{3,1}
with
W_{4}
. On the RHS, we encounter
W_{4}
itself, with OPE coefficient

\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{3}}_{3,1}\times W_{4}=-\frac{108(\nu-16)(\nu+1)g_{3}}{5(\nu-11)(\nu-4)(\nu-2)}W_{4}+\dots

(55)

The OPE coefficient
has a finite, non-zero
limit for
\nu=9
.
(Here we assume
g_{3}\neq 0
.)
Null states form an ideal.
For
\nu=9
,
we see that a non-zero multiple of
W_{4}
appears
on the RHS of the OPE with the null state
\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{3}}_{3,1}
with something.
We conclude that
W_{4}
must be null.
By a similar token, we consider the OPE
10
10

10

Since we now know that
W_{4}
is null, one might think to consider the OPE
W_{3}\times W_{4}
to argue that
W_{5}
is null.
However, the OPE coefficient

c_{34}{}^{5}
depends not only on
g_{3}
but also on
g_{4}
and
g_{5}
,

(c_{34}{}^{5})^{2}=\frac{60(\nu-16)(\nu+1)g_{3}g_{4}}{(\nu-4)(\nu-1)(\nu+5)g_{5}}\ .

(56)

This is why we have to resort to the more complicated OPE
\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{3}}_{3,1}\times W_{5}
,
in which
W_{5}
appears with a coefficient that depends on
g_{3}
and
\nu
only.

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{3}}_{3,1}\times W_{5}

\displaystyle=-\frac{18\sqrt{\frac{3}{5}}\sqrt{\nu-16}\sqrt{\nu-9}\sqrt{\nu}\sqrt{g_{3}}\sqrt{g_{5}}}{(\nu-11)(\nu-4)\sqrt{\nu+5}}W_{3}

(57)

\displaystyle-\frac{6\left(5\nu^{3}-103\nu^{2}-460\nu-864\right)g_{3}}{(\nu-11)(\nu-4)(\nu-2)(\nu+5)}W_{5}+\dots

The coefficient of
W_{5}

is finite and non-zero for

\nu=9
. We learn that
W_{5}
must be null.
As a consistency check,
we see that the coefficient of
W_{3}
goes to zero for
\nu=9
(it also has a factor
\sqrt{g_{5}}
).
This is consistent with
W_{3}
being non-null for
\nu=9
.
Next, we consider

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{3}}_{3,1}\times W_{6}

\displaystyle=-\frac{42\sqrt{6}\sqrt{\nu-25}\sqrt{\nu-16}\sqrt{\nu}\sqrt{\nu+1}g_{3}\sqrt{g_{6}}}{5(\nu-11)(\nu-4)\sqrt{\nu^{2}+15\nu+8}\sqrt{g_{4}}}W_{4}

(58)

\displaystyle-\frac{18\left(11\nu^{4}-292\nu^{3}-3223\nu^{2}-10304\nu-2560\right)g_{3}}{5(\nu-11)(\nu-4)(\nu-2)\left(\nu^{2}+15\nu+8\right)}W_{6}+\dots

The coefficient of
W_{6}
is finite and non-zero
for
\nu=9
. Hence,
W_{6}
must be null.
We expect that this line of reasoning can be extended to prove that
W_{7}
,
W_{8}
, etc. are also null.

Let us now discuss other consistency checks of our proposal that
W_{p}
with
p\geq 4
is null.
As noted above, null states form an ideal.
Thus,
all OPE coefficients of the form

c_{3p}{}^{3}
,
p\geq 4
,
and
c_{pq}{}^{3}
,
p,q\geq 4

must go to zero as
\nu\rightarrow 9
.
Indeed, we verify that this is the case
for
c_{34}{}^{3}
,
c_{36}{}^{3}
,
c_{45}{}^{3}
. Now
c_{36}{}^{3}=0
by “gauge fixing” (
27
).
In contrast,

c_{34}{}^{3}
,
c_{45}{}^{3}

are non-trivial functions of
\nu

(see Tables
2
and

6
) repeated here for convenience,

\displaystyle c_{34}{}^{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{12(\nu-9)\nu}{\nu+1}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{45}{}^{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{20(\nu-16)(\nu-9)\nu}{\nu+5}\ ,

(59)

They indeed have a zero
at
\nu=9
.

Upon modding out
W_{p}
’s with
p\geq 4
, the W-algebra
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

reduces to a VOA
with small
\mathcal{N}=4
supersymmetry,
central charge
c=-24
,
and strong generators

\mathbb{J}
,
\mathbb{W}_{3}
.
These features match those of the VOA

\mathcal{V}(A_{2})
.
Another check is provided by the
composite super Virasoro primary
operators

\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}
,

\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}
.
In the VOA
\mathcal{V}(A_{2})
,
the composite
\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}
is null, while

\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}
is non-null.
(This follows from the free-field realization reviewed in Appendix

B
.)
We reproduce these features starting from
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
. Indeed,
using the explicit data from
the bootstrap analysis of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
, we can compute
the 2-point function
coefficients of
\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}
,

\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}
with themselves.
We have already reported
the norm squared of
\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{3}}_{3,1}

in (
54
). That of
\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{3}}_{3,3}
reads

\langle\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{3}}_{3,3}\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{3}}_{3,3}\rangle=\frac{2\nu\left(\nu^{2}+15\nu+8\right)}{(\nu-4)(\nu+3)(\nu+7)}g_{3}^{2}\ .

(60)

As anticipated, this has a non-zero finite
limit for
\nu\rightarrow 9
.

3.2.3
The case
N=4

It is straightforward to check that all OPE coefficient in Tables
2
-
9

remain finite in the limit
\nu\rightarrow 16
. Moreover, we see from
(
26
) that the operators
W_{5}
,
W_{6}
, etc. become null.

We can also argue that
W_{5}

must be null in a way independent on any choice of normalization.
To this end we consider again the composite operator

\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{3}}_{3,1}
with norm squared
(
54
). We see that this operator is null for
\nu=16
.
In the OPE
(
55
), the coefficient of
W_{4}
goes to zero for
\nu=16
,
so we cannot draw any conclusion from this OPE.
This is consistent with
W_{4}
being non-null.
In the OPE (
57
),
the coefficient of

W_{5}
is finite and non-zero
for
\nu=16
. We learn that
W_{5}
must be null.
As a sanity check,
W_{3}
can be non-null, because its coefficient in
(
57
) is zero for
\nu=16
.
In a similar way,
the coefficient of
W_{6}
in
(
58
) is finite and non-zero for

\nu=16
, demonstrating that
W_{6}
must be null. As another sanity check,
the coefficient of
W_{4}

in (
58
) goes to zero
for
\nu=16
, compatibly with
W_{4}
being non-null.
We expect that similar arguments can be used to infer that
W_{7}
,
W_{8}
, etc. are null.

We now turn to other consistency checks of our claim that
W_{p}
with
p\geq 5
is null.
All OPE coefficients of the form

c_{rp}{}^{s}
(
p\geq 5
,
r,s\in\{3,4\}
),
and
c_{pq}{}^{r}
, (
p,q\geq 5
,

)
must go to zero as
\nu\rightarrow 16
.
We verify this
explicitly
for the OPE coefficients

c_{35}{}^{4}
,

c_{45}{}^{3}
,

c_{46}{}^{4}
,

c_{55}{}^{4}
,
which are given by

\displaystyle c_{35}{}^{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{15(\nu-16)(\nu+1)}{\nu+5}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{45}{}^{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{20(\nu-16)(\nu-9)\nu}{\nu+5}\ ,

(61)

\displaystyle c_{46}{}^{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{24(\nu-25)(\nu-16)\nu(\nu+1)}{\nu^{2}+15\nu+8}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{55}{}^{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{25(\nu-16)\left(\nu^{3}-32\nu^{2}-77\nu+36\right)}{(\nu+5)^{2}}\ ,

They all vanish for
\nu=16
.

Upon modding out
W_{p}
’s with
p\geq 5
, the W-algebra
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

reduces to a VOA
with small
\mathcal{N}=4
supersymmetry,
central charge
c=-45
,
and strong generators

\mathbb{J}
,
\mathbb{W}_{3}
,

\mathbb{W}_{4}
.
These features match those of the VOA

\mathcal{V}(A_{3})
.
Another check is provided by the
composite super Virasoro primary
operators

\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}
,

\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}
.
In the VOA
\mathcal{V}(A_{3})
,
the composite
\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}
is null, while

\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}

We reproduce these features starting from
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
. Indeed,

)

,

Next, we verify that

\displaystyle W_{4}\times W_{4}

\displaystyle=g_{4}\text{1}+c_{44}{}^{4}W_{4}+c_{44}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}\ ,

with OPE coefficients

\displaystyle=\tfrac{4\sqrt{7}}{\sqrt{85}}\tfrac{g_{3}}{\sqrt{g_{4}}}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{34}{}^{3}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{4\sqrt{7}}{\sqrt{85}}\sqrt{g_{4}}\ ,

(63)

\displaystyle c_{44}{}^{4}

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{11\sqrt{5}}{3\sqrt{119}}\sqrt{g_{4}}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{44}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{17}{28}\tfrac{g_{4}}{g_{3}}\ .

These are derived from the free-field
realization reviewed in Appendix

B
.

All these four OPE coefficients are indeed exactly reproduced

of

,

in Tables

-
9

and
g_{p}
in (

3.2.4
The case
N=5

It is straightforward to check that all OPE coefficients in Tables

-

remain finite in the limit

To argue that

(

First, we observe that the composite

\langle\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{4}}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{4}}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{3}{2}}\rangle=\frac{6(\nu-25)(\nu-16)(\nu+1)}{5(\nu-13)(\nu-4)(\nu-1)}g_{3}g_{4}\ .

(64)

We see that it becomes null for
\nu=25
.
Next, we consider the OPE

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{W_{3}W_{4}}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{3}{2}}\times W_{5}=-\frac{24\sqrt{\frac{3}{5}}(\nu-25)\sqrt{\nu-16}(\nu+1)(2\nu+3)\sqrt{g_{3}}\sqrt{g_{5}}}{(\nu-13)\sqrt{\nu-9}(\nu-4)(\nu-1)\sqrt{\nu}\sqrt{\nu+5}}W_{4}

(65)

\displaystyle-\frac{4\sqrt{\frac{3}{5}}\sqrt{\nu-16}(\nu+1)\left(13\nu^{3}-454\nu^{2}-2407\nu-4352\right)\sqrt{g_{3}}g_{4}}{\left(\nu-4\right)\left(\nu-1\right)(\nu-13)\sqrt{\nu-9}\sqrt{\nu}(\nu+5)^{3/2}\sqrt{g_{5}}}W_{6}+\dots\ .

The coefficient of
W_{6}

.
We conclude that

In contrast, the coefficient of

We expect that similar arguments can be used to prove that
W_{7}
,
W_{8}
, etc. are null.

Null states should form an ideal.
Thus,
as a consistency check,
all OPE
coefficients of the form

c_{rp}{}^{s}
(

,
r,s\in\{3,4,5\}
),
and

, (

,

)
must go to zero as
\nu\rightarrow 25
.
The OPE coefficients
c_{36}{}^{3}
and
c_{37}{}^{4}

are of this form,
and have been set to zero in (
27
).
A more interesting check is provided by the OPE coefficient

c_{46}{}^{4}=\frac{24(\nu-25)(\nu-16)\nu(\nu+1)}{\nu^{2}+15\nu+8}\ ,

(66)

which indeed vanishes for
\nu=25
.

Upon modding out
W_{p}
’s with

, the W-algebra
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

with small
\mathcal{N}=4
supersymmetry,

c=-72
,
and strong generators

\mathbb{J}
,
\mathbb{W}_{3}
,

\mathbb{W}_{4}
,

\mathbb{W}_{5}
.
These features match those of the VOA

\mathcal{V}(A_{4})
.
Another check is provided by the
composite super Virasoro primary operator

\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{3}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}
.
In the VOA
\mathcal{V}(A_{4})
,

(This follows from the free-field
realization.)
We reproduce this feature

).

Next, we verify that

of the VOA

.

The

of the VOA
