are derived in Appendix

B
from the free-field realization. The result reads

\displaystyle W_{3}\times W_{3}

\displaystyle=g_{3}\text{1}+c_{33}{}^{4}W_{4}\ ,

(67)

\displaystyle W_{3}\times W_{4}

\displaystyle=c_{34}{}^{3}W_{3}+c_{34}{}^{5}W_{5}\ ,

\displaystyle W_{3}\times W_{5}

\displaystyle=c_{35}{}^{4}W_{4}+c_{35}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}+c_{35}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}\ ,

\displaystyle W_{4}\times W_{4}

\displaystyle=g_{4}\text{1}+c_{44}{}^{4}W_{4}+c_{44}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}+c_{44}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}\ ,

\displaystyle W_{4}\times W_{5}

\displaystyle=c_{45}{}^{3}W_{3}+c_{45}{}^{5}W_{5}+c_{45}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{1}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}}\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{1}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}+c_{45}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{5}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}}\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{5}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}+c_{45}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{7}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}}\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{7}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}\ ,

\displaystyle W_{5}\times W_{5}

\displaystyle=g_{5}\text{1}+c_{55}{}^{4}W_{4}+c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}+c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}+\overline{c}_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,0}^{W_{4}W_{4}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,0}^{W_{4}W_{4}}

\displaystyle+\overline{c}_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{5}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{5}}+\overline{c}_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{4}W_{4}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{4}W_{4}}+c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{3}W_{5}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{3}W_{5}}+c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{4}W_{4}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{4}W_{4}}\ ,

where the OPE coefficients are given as

\displaystyle c_{33}{}^{4}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{10}{\sqrt{91}}\tfrac{g_{3}}{\sqrt{g_{4}}}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{34}{}^{4}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{10}{\sqrt{91}}\sqrt{g_{4}}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{34}{}^{5}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\sqrt{13}}{\sqrt{14}}\tfrac{\sqrt{g_{3}}\sqrt{g_{4}}}{\sqrt{g_{5}}}\ ,

(68)

\displaystyle c_{35}{}^{4}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\sqrt{13}}{\sqrt{14}}\tfrac{\sqrt{g_{3}}\sqrt{g_{5}}}{\sqrt{g_{4}}}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{35}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{23}{30\sqrt{2}}\tfrac{\sqrt{g_{5}}}{\sqrt{g_{3}}}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{35}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{5\sqrt{2}}\tfrac{\sqrt{g_{5}}}{\sqrt{g_{3}}}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{44}{}^{4}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{67}{15\sqrt{91}}\sqrt{g_{4}}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{44}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{39}{50}\tfrac{g_{4}}{g_{3}}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{44}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{13}{25}\tfrac{g_{4}}{g_{3}}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{45}{}^{3}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\sqrt{13}}{\sqrt{14}}\tfrac{\sqrt{g_{4}}\sqrt{g_{5}}}{\sqrt{g_{3}}}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{45}{}^{5}

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{87}{10\sqrt{91}}\sqrt{g_{4}}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{45}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{1}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}}

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{37}{30\sqrt{2}}\tfrac{\sqrt{g_{5}}}{\sqrt{g_{3}}}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{45}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{5}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{3}{10\sqrt{2}}\tfrac{\sqrt{g_{5}}}{\sqrt{g_{3}}}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{45}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{7}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{23}{30\sqrt{2}}\tfrac{\sqrt{g_{5}}}{\sqrt{g_{3}}}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{55}{}^{4}

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{87}{10\sqrt{91}}\tfrac{g_{5}}{\sqrt{g_{4}}}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,1}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{77}{1800}\tfrac{g_{5}}{g_{3}}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{3,3}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{173}{300}\tfrac{g_{5}}{g_{3}}\ ,

\displaystyle\overline{c}_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,0}^{W_{4}W_{4}}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{116}{351}\tfrac{g_{5}}{g_{4}}\ ,

\displaystyle\overline{c}_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{5}}}

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{3981}{17875\sqrt{2}}\tfrac{\sqrt{g_{5}}}{\sqrt{g_{3}}}\ ,

\displaystyle\overline{c}_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{4}W_{4}}}

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{58012}{83655}\tfrac{g_{5}}{g_{4}}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{3}W_{5}}}

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\tfrac{\sqrt{g_{5}}}{\sqrt{g_{3}}}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{4}W_{4}}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{25}{39}\tfrac{\sqrt{g_{5}}}{\sqrt{g_{4}}}\ .

We notice the absence of

\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{3}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}
,

\mathcal{C}_{4,0}^{W_{3}W_{3}}
,
and

\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{3}}
.
This is because the following three
operators are null in

,

\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{3}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}\ ,\quad\mathcal{C}_{4,0}^{W_{3}W_{3}}+\tfrac{25}{13}\tfrac{g_{3}}{g_{4}}\mathcal{C}_{4,0}^{W_{4}W_{4}}\ ,\quad\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{3}}+\tfrac{315\sqrt{2}}{143}\tfrac{\sqrt{g_{3}}}{\sqrt{g_{5}}}\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{5}}+\tfrac{3500}{1859}\tfrac{g_{3}}{g_{4}}\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{4}W_{4}}\ .

(69)

The fact that these operators
are null
in
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

for
\nu=25

is confirmed from the 2-point functions of composite operators
recorded in Appendix

A.5
.

To compare the OPEs
(
68
)
of the VOA
\mathcal{V}(A_{4})

to the OPEs of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

in Table
1
,
we have to take into
account that the states in
(
69
) are null.
More precisely,
we
set the three quantities
in (
69
) to zero,
solve for

\mathcal{C}_{\frac{7}{2},\frac{3}{2}}^{W_{3}W_{4}}
,

\mathcal{C}_{4,0}^{W_{3}W_{3}}
,

\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{3}}
,
and plug their expressions
in the OPEs in
Table
1
.
We obtain the same structure
as in (
67
)
with the identifications

\displaystyle\overline{c}_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,0}^{W_{4}W_{4}}}

\displaystyle=c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,0}^{W_{4}W_{4}}}-\tfrac{25}{13}\tfrac{g_{3}}{g_{4}}c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,0}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\ ,

(70)

\displaystyle\overline{c}_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{5}}}

\displaystyle=c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{5}}}-\tfrac{315\sqrt{2}}{143}\tfrac{\sqrt{g_{3}}}{\sqrt{g_{5}}}c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\ ,

\displaystyle\overline{c}_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{4}W_{4}}}

\displaystyle=c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{4}W_{4}}}-\tfrac{3500}{1859}\tfrac{g_{3}}{g_{4}}c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,2}^{W_{3}W_{3}}}\ .

We are now in a position
to verify that the OPE
coefficients of
\mathcal{V}(A_{4})

in (
68
) follow from
the OPE coefficients of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

taking the limit
\nu\rightarrow 25
. It is straightforward to
take the OPE data in Tables

2
-
9

and verify that we indeed reproduce
all OPE coefficients
in (
68
), with the exception of

c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{3}W_{5}}}\ ,\qquad c_{55}{}^{\mathcal{C}_{4,4}^{W_{4}W_{4}}}\ .

(71)

This is because these OPE coefficients in
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

have not been determined yet
at the current stage of the bootstrap analysis.
Our expectation is that they would also be reproduced, if we were to
push the bootstrap of
W_{p_{1}}\times W_{p_{2}}
OPEs in
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
to higher values of
p_{1}+p_{2}
.

4
Connection with 4d
\mathcal{N}=4

SU(N)
super Yang-Mills

In this section we aim
to collect further
evidence that, upon setting

c=3(1-N^{2})
, equivalently

\nu=N^{2}
, the simple quotient
of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
is isomorphic
to the VOA associated to 4d
\mathcal{N}=4

SU(N)
super Yang-Mills (SYM),
and that the
\mathfrak{R}
-filtration
is identified with the
R
-filtration
coming from 4d physics
Beem:2013sza
;
Beem:2017ooy
.
To achieve this goal, we will perform
a counting of
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)

primary in
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
and compare it against:

•

the Macdonald index of 4d
\mathcal{N}=4

SU(N)
SYM;

•

the Hilbert series of the Hall-Littlewood chiral ring of 4d
\mathcal{N}=4

SU(N)
SYM;

•

the BRST construction
of the VOA associated to 4d
\mathcal{N}=4

SU(N)
SYM.

We will test
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
primaries with conformal weight
h\leq 4
,
for any value of
N
.

Before going into the details of our tests, we briefly review the
map from 4d
\mathcal{N}=2
SCFTs and VOAs and
the notion of
R
-filtration.

4.1
Brief review of the 4d/2d map

4.1.1
Some general features

The map of
Beem:2013sza
associates to a 4d
\mathcal{N}=2
superconformal field theory
a VOA obtained by passing to the cohomology of a suitable nilpotent fermionic
generator
\mathbbmtt{Q}
in the superconformal algebra.
The choice of fermionic generator
\mathbbmtt{Q}

selects a 2d plane
w=\bar{w}=0

inside 4d spacetime with complex coordinates

(z,\bar{z},w,\bar{w})
.
Non-trivial cohomology classes
of local operators inserted at the origin

(z,\bar{z},w,\bar{w})=(0,0,0,0)

admit canonical
representatives given by Schur operators.
Let
(E,j_{1},j_{2},R,r)
denote the quantum numbers
of the 4d
\mathcal{N}=2
superconformal algebra
\mathfrak{sl}(4|2)
.
Here
E
is the conformal dimension, while

j_{1}
,
j_{2}
,
R
,
r
are the eigenvalues with respect to the Cartan generators
of
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{2}\oplus\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{R}\oplus\mathfrak{gl}(1)_{r}
, respectively.
A Schur operator is a highest weight state of
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{2}\oplus\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{R}\oplus\mathfrak{gl}(1)_{r}
,
and its quantum numbers satisfy

E-(j_{1}+j_{2})-2R=0\ ,\qquad r+j_{1}-j_{2}=0\ .

(72)

A Schur operator inserted at the origin can
be moved to any other point
(z,\bar{z})
on the VOA plane

w=\bar{w}=0
by means of a twisted translation,
see
Beem:2013sza
for details.
A twisted translated Schur operator
transforms has
conformal weight

h=E-R=\tfrac{1}{2}(E+j_{1}+j_{2})\ .

(73)

The OPE of twisted translated Schur operators
is defined by taking the 4d OPE and passing to the cohomology
of the supercharge
\mathbbmtt{Q}
. One obtains a meromorphic OPE
which endows the vector space of Schur operators with the
structure of a VOA.

For the purpose of enumeration, it is sufficient to focus on those Schur operators that are
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{z}

primaries.
In terms of (unitary) 4d
\mathcal{N}=2
superconformal multiplets,
these Schur operators are found in short multiplets of
type
\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{R}
,
\mathcal{D}_{R(0,j_{2})}
,

\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{R(j_{1},0)}
, and

\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{R(j_{1},j_{2})}
, in the notation of
Dolan:2002zh
.
Table
10
summarizes how Schur operators enter such multiplets and how their quantum numbers are related to those of the superconformal primary operator in the multiplet.

4d
\mathcal{N}=2
multiplet

Schur operator

structure

h

r

R

\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{R^{\prime}}

\Psi^{1\dots 1}

R^{\prime}

0

R^{\prime}

\mathcal{D}_{R^{\prime}(0,j_{2})}

\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\dot{+}}^{1}\Psi^{1\dots 1}_{\dot{+}\dots\dot{+}}

R^{\prime}+j_{2}+1

j_{2}+\frac{1}{2}

R^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}

\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{R^{\prime}(j_{1},0)}

\mathcal{Q}_{+}^{1}\Psi^{1\dots 1}_{+\dots+}

R^{\prime}+j_{1}+1

R^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}

\mathcal{Q}_{+}^{1}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\dot{+}}^{1}\Psi^{1\dots 1}_{+\dots+\dot{+}\dots\dot{+}}

j_{2}-j_{1}

R^{\prime}+1

Table 10:
4d
\mathcal{N}=2
superconformal multiplet containing a Schur operator.
For each multiplet we give the schematic form of the Schur operator in terms of the
superconformal primary
\Psi
of the multiplet.
We also report the quantum numbers
(h,r,R)
of the Schur operator
in terms of the quantum numbers of the multiplet.
(We use
R^{\prime}
for the
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{R}
quantum number
of the multiplet.)

The OPE structure on the vector space of Schur operators
preserves the quantum numbers
h
and
r
, but it does not preserve the
quantum number
R
. There exists, however, a filtration of the VOA
which encodes
R

Beem:2013sza
;
Beem:2017ooy
. More precisely, let us define

\mathcal{V}_{h,r,R}=\{\text{Schur operators with definite $(h,r,R)\in\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\times\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\times\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}$}\}\ .

(74)

We also introduce

\mathcal{F}_{h,r,R}=\bigoplus_{k\geq 0}\mathcal{V}_{h,r,R-k}\ .

(75)

The subspaces
\mathcal{F}_{h,r,R}
furnish an increasing filtration
of the space
\mathcal{V}_{h,r}
of Schur operators of definite
(h,r)
.
This filtration enjoys the following additional properties.
The unit operator satisfies
\text{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{0,0,0}
.
Suppose
\mathcal{O}_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{h_{1},r_{1},R_{1}}

and
\mathcal{O}_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{h_{2},r_{2},R_{2}}
.
Parametrize the full

\mathcal{O}_{1}\times\mathcal{O}_{2}
OPE
as

\mathcal{O}_{1}(z)\mathcal{O}_{2}(0)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{z^{n}}\{\mathcal{O}_{1}\mathcal{O}_{2}\}_{n}(0)\ .

(76)

Then, one has

\displaystyle\{\mathcal{O}_{1}\mathcal{O}_{2}\}_{0}

\displaystyle\in\mathcal{F}_{h_{1}+h_{2},r_{1}+r_{2},R_{1}+R_{2}}

\displaystyle\text{(normal ordered product)}\ ,

(77)

\displaystyle\{\mathcal{O}_{1}\mathcal{O}_{2}\}_{1}

\displaystyle\in\mathcal{F}_{h_{1}+h_{2}-1,r_{1}+r_{2},R_{1}+R_{2}-1}

\displaystyle\text{(simple pole in the OPE)}\ .

Given the filtration
\mathcal{F}_{h,r,R}
of
\mathcal{V}_{h,r}
, the associated graded
is defined as

{\rm gr}\mathcal{V}_{h,r}=\bigoplus_{R\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathcal{G}_{h,r,R}\ ,\qquad\mathcal{G}_{h,r,R}=\mathcal{F}_{h,r,R}/\mathcal{F}_{h,r,R-1}\ .

(78)

As a vector space,
{\rm gr}\mathcal{V}_{h,r}
is isomorphic to

\mathcal{V}_{h,r}
.

Without knowledge of the
R
-filtration
it is in general not possible to invert the map
from 4d
\mathcal{N}=2
superconformal multiplets
to
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{z}
primaries in the VOA.
If we have access to the quantum number
R
, however,
we can reconstruct unambiguously the 4d origin of
a 2d
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{z}
primary:

\begin{array}[]{l}\text{$\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{z}$ primary}\\
\text{with $(h,R,r)$}\end{array}\qquad\text{comes from}\qquad\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{R^{\prime}(j_{1},j_{2})}\;\;\text{with}\;\;\begin{array}[]{rcl}R^{\prime}&=&R-1\ ,\\
2j_{1}&=&h-R-r-1\ ,\\
2j_{2}&=&h-R+r-1\ .\end{array}

(79)

Formally, we can still apply (
79
)
in the the special cases in which

h=R+r
and/or
h=R-r
,
provided we make use of the identifications

Dolan:2002zh

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{R^{\prime}(j_{1},-\frac{1}{2})}

\displaystyle\cong\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{R^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}(j_{1},0)}\ ,

(80)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{R^{\prime}(-\frac{1}{2},j_{2})}

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{D}_{R^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}(0,j_{2})}\ ,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{R^{\prime}(-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2})}

\displaystyle\cong\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{R^{\prime}+1}\ .

4.1.2
The case of 4d
\mathcal{N}=4
super Yang-Mills

We are interested in studying Schur operators in 4d
\mathcal{N}=4

SU(N)

super Yang-Mills theory.
In this case, due to the additional supersymmetry
compared to a generic
\mathcal{N}=2
theory,
Schur operators are
organized in
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
multiplets.
We denote the quantum numbers of
the 4d
\mathcal{N}=4
superconformal algebra as

(E,j_{1},j_{2},[q_{1},p,q_{2}])
,
with
E
,
j_{1}
,
j_{2}
as in the
\mathcal{N}=2
case,
and
[q_{1},p,q_{2}]
Dynkin labels of
\mathfrak{sl}(4)
.
In Table
11

we list the (unitary) 4d
\mathcal{N}=4
superconformal multiplets
that give rise to
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
multiplets
of Schur operator.
We adopt the notation of
Dolan:2002zh
for 4d multiplets.
For each
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
multiplet
we give
the following quantum numbers of its
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
primary:
conformal weight
h
, spin
j
under
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}
,

r
and
R
charges.
We refer the reader to Section 6.1.4 of
Bonetti:2018fqz

for further details.

If we know the quantum numbers
(h,j,r,R)
of
a
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
primary Schur operator,
we can unambiguously reconstruct its origin in four dimensions.
In the generic case
h\neq R+r
,
h\neq R-r
,
from Table
11

we find that

\begin{array}[]{l}\text{$\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)$ primary}\\
\text{with $(h,j,r,R)$}\end{array}\qquad\text{comes from}\qquad\mathcal{C}_{[q_{1},p,q_{2}](j_{1},j_{2})}\;\;\text{with}\;\;\begin{array}[]{rcl}p&=&2j\ ,\\
q_{1}&=&R+r-j-1\ ,\\
q_{2}&=&R-r-j-1\ ,\\
2j_{1}&=&h-R-r-1\ ,\\
2j_{2}&=&h-R+r-1\ .\end{array}

(81)

Formally, we can still apply (
81
)
in the the special cases in which

h=R+r
and/or
h=R-r
,
provided we make use of the identifications

Dolan:2002zh

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{[q_{1},p,q_{2}](j_{1},-\frac{1}{2})}

\displaystyle\cong\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{[q_{1},p,q_{2}+1](j_{1},0)}\ ,

(82)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{[q_{1},p,q_{2}](-\frac{1}{2},j_{2})}

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{D}_{[q_{1}+1,p,q_{2}](0,j_{2})}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{[q_{1},p,q_{2}](-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2})}

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{B}_{[q_{1}+1,p,q_{1}+1](0,0)}\ .

We close this subsection with a remark on the
r
charge. The small
\mathcal{N}=4
superconformal algebra enjoys an

\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{\rm out}
outer automorphism, under which the supercurrents

G
,
\widetilde{G}
transform as a doublet. The 4d quantum number
r
is identified with the Cartan eigenvalue
of
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{\rm out}
,
in conventions in which

G
,
\widetilde{G}
have charge
+\frac{1}{2}
,
-\frac{1}{2}
respectively.

4d
\mathcal{N}=4
multiplet

\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
multiplet

h

j

r

R

\mathcal{B}_{[q,p,q]}

\frac{1}{2}p+q

\frac{1}{2}p+q

\mathcal{D}_{[q_{1},p,q_{2}](0,j_{2})}

\frac{1}{2}p+q_{1}

\frac{1}{2}p

\frac{1}{2}(q_{1}-q_{2}-1)

\frac{1}{2}(p+q_{1}+q_{2}+1)

\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{[q_{1},p,q_{2}](j_{1},0)}

\frac{1}{2}p+q_{2}

\frac{1}{2}p

\frac{1}{2}(q_{1}-q_{2}+1)

\frac{1}{2}(p+q_{1}+q_{2}+1)

\mathcal{C}_{[q_{1},p,q_{2}](j_{1},j_{2})}

\frac{1}{2}(q+q_{1}+q_{2})+(j_{1}+j_{2})+2

\frac{1}{2}p

\frac{1}{2}(q_{1}-q_{2})

\frac{1}{2}(p+q_{1}+q_{2}+2)

Table 11:
Summary of 4d
\mathcal{N}=4
superconformal multiplet
yielding
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
multiplets of Schur operators.
For each
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
multiplet we
give the quantum numbers of its
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)

primary operator.
Recall that: a multiplet of type
\mathcal{D}_{[q_{1},p,q_{2}](0,j_{2})}

satisfies
q_{1}-q_{2}=2(j_{2}+1)
;
a multiplet of type
\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{[q_{1},p,q_{2}](j_{1},0)}

satisfies
q_{1}-q_{2}=-2(j_{1}+1)
;
a multiplet of type
\mathcal{C}_{[q_{1},p,q_{2}](j_{1},j_{2})}

satisfies
q_{1}-q_{2}=2(j_{2}-j_{1})
.

4.2
Low-lying
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
primaries in
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

In this subsection we present a detailed counting of

\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
primaries
with conformal weight
h\leq 4

in the W-algebra
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}
.
Besides
h
, we keep track of the following quantum numbers:
the
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{y}
spin
j
, the

\mathfrak{gl}(1)_{r}
charge
r
,
and the
\mathfrak{R}
weight,
as defined in Section
2.4
.

Our main results are collected in
Table
12
.
Some comments are in order.
For each value of
(h,j)

we give the value of the quantity

\sum_{\mathfrak{R},r}n_{h,j,r,\mathfrak{R}}\;x^{2r}\xi^{\mathfrak{R}}\ ,

(83)

where

n_{h,j,r,\mathfrak{R}}
is the number of linearly independent

\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
primaries
with quantum numbers
(h,j,r,\mathfrak{R})
.
Thus, here
\xi
is a fugacity for the

\mathfrak{R}
-weight,
and
x
a fugacity for the
r
charge.
In Table
12
we also make use of the shorthand notation

\mathfrak{N}=\xi^{3}+\xi^{2}+\xi^{7/2}\chi_{1}(x)\ ,

(84)

where
\chi_{1}(x)
is the character of the 2-dimesional irrep of
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{\rm out}
,

\chi_{1}(x)=x+x^{-1}\ .

(85)

Under the column ‘generic
\nu
’
we give the value of
(
83
) for a generic value of the central charge.
In the other columns
we give the value of
(
83
)
for the simple quotient of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

with
\nu=N^{2}
, equivalently

c=3(1-N^{2})
, for
N=2,3,\dots
.

Some additional remarks:

•

Up to
h=7/2
all
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
primaries are neutral under
\mathfrak{gl}(1)_{r}
.
The lightest
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
primaries with non-trivial
r
-charge appear at
(h,j)=(4,1)
for generic
\nu
or
N\geq 4
. (This is signaled by the presence of the quantity
\mathfrak{N}
, which contains
\chi_{1}(x)
.) These
\mathfrak{psl}(2|2)
primaries
form a doublet of
\mathfrak{sl}(2)_{\rm out}
and
are Grassmann odd.

•

The entries of the column
‘generic
\nu
’ can be extracted easily
from a partition function, as explained
below in Section
4.2.1
. In contrast, the entries of the columns
for
\nu=N^{2}

require a careful analysis of
the null states that appear in
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\text{s,s}}

for those values of
\nu
.
Some details are reported in Appendix

D
.

•

For fixed
(h,j)
, considering

\nu=N^{2}
for increasing values of
N
,
we observe that
degeneracies
n_{h,j,r,\mathfrak{R}}

are non-decreasing. From some value of
N
onward, they stabilize and become equal to the
degeneracies for generic
\nu
.
We have used the color blue to highlight those entries that have stabilized.
We also observe that,
for
N\geq 2h
, all states with conformal weight
h
have stabilized
(some stabilize even before, for values of
N
smaller than
2h
).

\nu=N^{2}

with
N
equal to

j

\nu

3\xi^{3}+7\xi^{4}

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}3\xi^{3}+8\xi^{4}

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}3\xi^{3}+8\xi^{4}

\xi^{3}+2\xi^{4}+\mathfrak{N}

0

\xi^{3}

\xi^{3}+\mathfrak{N}

\xi^{3}+\xi^{4}+\mathfrak{N}

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xi^{3}+2\xi^{4}+\mathfrak{N}

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xi^{3}+2\xi^{4}+\mathfrak{N}

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xi^{3}+2\xi^{4}+\mathfrak{N}

\xi^{2}+2\xi^{3}+4\xi^{4}

\xi^{2}+\xi^{3}+\xi^{4}
